leaves out is not in the data at all, so Bloom's defaults hold.

`Rail(...items)` is Bloom's `Rail`: each `RailItem(label, glyph)` an item
whose glyph is a view the app draws in its own ink
(`RailItem('Home', Icon(RiHome5Line).foreground('muted')).activeIcon(Icon(RiHome5Fill).foreground('primary'))`,
as apps hand Bloom's rail their elements), with `.selection([id, setId])` (a
binding: Bloom's `activeId`, and its `onSelect` writing it), `.header`, `.footer` and `.width(space)`.

`PageFooter(...children)` is Bloom's `PageFooter`: its children, then its
actions (`.toolbar(...)`), on its scrim. `.pinning('absolute' \| 'sticky' \|
'document' \| 'inline')` is its `position`, `.scrollEdge` its `scrim`,
`.ignoresSafeArea()` its `safeArea={false}`.

| | Android, OxyOS | iOS (not verified) | Web |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `Sidebar`, `Rail`, `PageFooter` | Bloom's | Bloom's (on iPad too; see below) | Bloom's |

On iPad the sidebar is Bloom's `AppShell` sidebar, by decision: the system's
(SwiftUI's `NavigationSplitView`) is not something `@expo/ui` offers (it has a
`List` in the sidebar style), and the shell's sidebar column, its width,
collapse and drawer are Bloom's `AppShell`.

### Command and SettingsModal

```ts
Command(
  CommandItem(t('compose')).icon(RiQuillPenLine).shortcut('⌘N').keywords('write').onPress(compose),
  CommandGroup(t('goTo'), CommandItem(t('settings')).onPress(navigate('/settings'))),
).presented([paletteOpen, setPaletteOpen])

SettingsModal(
  SettingsGroup(t('account'),
    SettingsItem(t('profile'), RiUserLine).id('profile').content(() => ProfilePage()),
    SettingsItem(t('signOut'), RiLogoutBoxRLine).onPress(signOut),
  ),
).presented([settingsOpen, setSettingsOpen]).selection([page, setPage])
```

`Command(...rows)` is Bloom's `Command` palette. Each `CommandItem(label)` is
a `CommandItem` (`.id`, its label by default; `.icon`, `.subtitle` its
description, `.keywords(...)`, `.shortcut`, `.disabled`, `.onPress` its
`onSelect`); a `CommandGroup(title, ...items)` puts its items under that
group, after the ungrouped ones, as Bloom orders them. The palette takes
`.presented([open, setOpen])` (a binding: Bloom's `open` and
`onOpenChange`), `.placeholder`, `.emptyLabel`, `.query([value, onChange])` and
`.filter(fn)`.

`SettingsModal(...groups)` is Bloom's `SettingsModal`. Each
`SettingsGroup(label, ...items)` is a navigation group; each
`SettingsItem(label, icon)` a row that opens its page when it has
`.content(() => view)` (built only while the page is shown; `.title` and
`.fullBleed` are the page's) or runs its `.onPress`. The modal takes
`.presented([open, setOpen])` (a binding; its `onClose` sets it to `false`),
`.selection([page, setPage])` (a binding: Bloom's `page` and its
`onPageChange`), `.initialView` and
`.presentation('bar' \| 'floating')`. A page's rows are views, and Bloom's own
settings rows are language nodes:

```ts
SettingsSection(t('account'),
  SettingsTextField(t('email'), [email, saveEmail]).icon(RiMailLine).keyboard('email-address').contentType('email'),
  SettingsDateField(t('birthday'), [birthday, setBirthday]).maxDate(today),
  SettingsSelect(t('autoplay'), useState<Autoplay>('wifi'), autoplayChoices),
  SettingsValue(device.id).icon(RiSmartphoneLine).muted(),
).description(t('accountHint')).trailing(Button(Text(t('manage'))).onPress(manage))
```

| Node | Bloom | Modifiers |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `SettingsSection(label, ...rows)` | `SettingsSection` (label `undefined`: an unlabelled card) | `.description`, `.trailing(view)` (its `action`), `.inset(space)`; its box is its `style` |
| `SettingsValue(text)` | `SettingsValueField` | `.icon(X)`, `.muted()`; its box is its `style` |
| `SettingsSelect(label, selection, choices)` | `SettingsSelect`: `value` and `onChange` from the binding, `items` the choices (`{ value, label }`) | `.compact(false)`, `.size`, `.disabled`, `.width(space)` (the web dropdown's); its box is the trigger's `style` |
| `SettingsTextField(label, text)` | `SettingsTextField`: it edits a draft and commits it on submit or blur, `value` and `onCommit` from the binding | `.icon`, `.placeholder`, `.keyboard`, `.contentType` (autofill), `.disabled`, `.savedToast(false)`, `.testID` |
| `SettingsDateField(label, date)` | `SettingsDateField`: `value` and `onChange` from the binding | `.minDate`, `.maxDate`, `.dateFormat(fn)`, `.savedToast(false)`, `.testID` |

A row's value is a `Binding` (`[value, setValue]`, what `useState` returns),
the inputs' one mechanism. A select's choices are Bloom's own `items` data,
since Bloom's settings select has no option rows of its own. A text field takes
the keyboards and autofill kinds Bloom's settings field offers (its
`keyboardType` and `autoComplete`), a subset of the inputs' `.keyboard` and
`.contentType` tokens. The two fields take only their own modifiers: Bloom's
take no style.

Both are Bloom's on every platform (on iOS not verified), with parity on
native (the modal through its native portal), the web and Chrome.

### Panels, deck, inspector and destinations

```ts
export default layout(function App() {
  const [details, setDetails] = useState(false);
  return AppLayout(Stack('(tabs)', Sheet('compose')))
    .destinations(
      Destination('index', '/').label(t('home')).icon(RiHome5Line).activeIcon(RiHome5Fill).bar(),
      Destination('notifications', '/notifications').label(t('alerts')).icon(RiNotification3Line).bar(),
      Destination('write', '/write').page(),
    )
    .shellLayout('workspace')
    .panels(
      Panel('main'),
      Panel('details', Details()).panelRole('inspector').label(t('details')).presented([details, setDetails]),
    )
    .primaryAction(RiQuillPenLine, t('compose'), 'write');
});
```

`AppLayout` says the rest of Bloom's `AppShell` in the language, over what
`.shell(navigator => options)` gives (which stays for the options the
language does not name):

| Modifier | Bloom |
| --- | --- |
| `.shellLayout('workspace' \| 'collection-detail' \| 'feed' \| 'deck' \| 'catalog' \| 'map-browser')` | `AppShell`'s `layout` (a deck: columns side by side that scroll across) |
| `.shellPresentation('continuous' \| 'framed' \| 'floating')` | `presentation` |
| `.panels(...panels)` | `panelLayout.panels`, over the options' own `panelLayout`; a `false`, `null` or `undefined` panel is none (a list beside the navigator only when the window splits) |
| `.panelAppearance('separated' \| 'joined' \| 'plain')` | `panelLayout.appearance`: a surface each, or one surface the panels share |
| `.primaryAction(icon, label, action)` | `primaryAction`, the bar's FAB and the sidebar's first row: `action` a destination's name (on iOS its prominent tab) or a handler, which the layout's body may choose by the page in front (on iOS a handler is the iPad sidebar's row; the system tab bar has no place for it) |
| `.sidebar(Sidebar(...))` | `sidebar`: the `Sidebar`'s own props (`.variant('rail')`, `.logo()`, `.searchable(false)`, its `.secondary()` rows, `.primaryAction(icon, label, onPress)`, the action it keeps whatever page is in front); the shell draws the destinations as its rows (rows of its own replace them, as Bloom's `sidebar.items` do) |
| `.activeDestination(name \| undefined)` | `sidebar.selected`: the destination the route in front belongs to (a conversation is the chats destination's); `undefined` is the page in front |
| `.navFrom('sm' \| 'md' \| 'lg' \| 'xl')` | `navFrom`: from that width the navigation is in flow, a drawer below it |
| `.contentMaxWidth(space)` | `contentMaxWidth`; `'none'` is no cap |
| `.reserveBottomBarSpace(false)` | `reserveBottomBarSpace={false}`: the bar floats over the content, and Bloom's scrollers clear it |
| `.destinations(...destinations)`, `.onReselect(name => …)` | a `TabPagerProvider` above the shell, with these `destinations` and its `onReselect` |

A `Panel(id, content?)` is an `AppShellPanelDefinition`: without content it
holds the layout's navigator, with it that view beside it. `.panelRole` is its
`role` (`main`, `collection`, `detail`, `inspector`, `context`, `canvas`,
each with Bloom's widths), and `.label`, `.width`, `.minWidth`, `.maxWidth`,
`.resizable`, `.resizeLabel` (its divider's name), `.hidden` (`visible={false}`),
`.keepMounted` and `.testID` its own. `.resizable([width, setWidth])` is a
width the app holds: Bloom's `width` and `onWidthChange`, the width it rests at
and the one a drag or an arrow key settles on. An **inspector** is a panel with the `inspector` role and
`.presented([open, setOpen])` (a binding: Bloom's `open`/`onOpenChange`,
and it needs a `.label`): a column at the end from `lg`, a floating sheet from
the end below it, as Bloom draws it.

A `Destination(name, href)` is Bloom's `AppDestination`: `.label`, `.icon`,
`.activeIcon`, `.badge`, `.bar()` (a tab of the bottom bar and a page),
`.page()` (a page without a tab), `.preload()`, `.sidebar(false)`,
`.sidebarAfter(name)`, `.onPress`, `.onLongPress`. With destinations the
layout draws Bloom's pager provider above the shell, so the app no longer
mounts one by hand; the router's `TabView()` is the pager itself (see
[Navigation](#navigation)).

`AppShellMenuButton()` is Bloom's drawer button for a page that draws its own
header (`Header(title).toolbarLeading(AppShellMenuButton().label(t('navigation.open')))`):
it hides while the sidebar is in flow and shows a close glyph while the drawer
is open.

Allo's chats shell, a rail and a list beside the conversation once the window
splits:

```ts
export default layout(function Chats() {
  const [listWidth, setListWidth] = useState(360);
  const split = useSplitLayout();
  return AppLayout(Embed(<ChatNavigator split={split} />))
    .primaryAction(RiChatNewLine, t('chat.new.title'), () => router.push('/new'))
    .sidebar(
      Sidebar(NavItem(t('settings.title'), RiSettings3Line).id('settings').secondary().onPress(settings.open))
        .variant('rail')
        .searchable(false)
        .primaryAction(RiChatNewLine, t('chat.new.title'), () => router.push('/new')),
    )
    .activeDestination(conversationId ? 'index' : undefined)
    .navFrom('md')
    .contentMaxWidth('none')
    .reserveBottomBarSpace(false)
    .panelAppearance('joined')
    .panels(
      split ? Panel('list', ConversationList()).resizable([listWidth, setListWidth]).minWidth('280').maxWidth('520').resizeLabel(t('chat.resize')) : null,
      Panel('detail'),
    );
});
```

The shell lays its panels out once it has measured itself, which neither jsdom
nor the test renderer does: the parity suite proves the shell's frame and
semantic layout there, the panels' definitions are unit tests, and Chrome
(`verify-parity-web.mjs`) compares the laid-out panels.

### The layout family

```ts
AutoHideBar(
  Toolbar(Button(Icon(RiBold)).label(t('bold'))).trailing(Button(Text(t('share')))),
  ScrollSurface(...messages.map((m) => Message({ m }).id(m.id)))
    .stickyHeader(DayHeader())
    .stickyFooter(Composer())
    .spacing('sm'),
)
  .collapsed(scrolledDown)
  .onReveal(() => setScrolledDown(false))
  .summary(page.host)
```

The rest of Bloom's `layout` family, each Bloom's own component:

| Node | Bloom | Modifiers |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `Toolbar(...tools)` | `Toolbar` | `.trailing(...)` (its `actions`, kept in reach at its end), `.accessory(view)` (its second row), `.size` (its controls'), `.testID` |
| `StickySection(...children)` | `StickySection` (docked under the header as the page scrolls past it, inside the app's `HeaderDockProvider`) | `.testID` |
| `ScrollArea(...children)` | `ScrollArea`: a scroll of its own (not the screen's) with Bloom's bar | `.contentMargins`, `.spacing`, `.axis('horizontal')`, `.scrollbar('scroll' \| 'hover' \| 'always' \| 'never')`, `.scrollbarSide('start' \| 'end')` (the web's; a native bar is the system's), `.size('sm' \| 'md')` (the bar's), `.scrollDismissesKeyboard`, `.keyboardTaps` |
| `ScrollSurface(...children)` | `ScrollSurface`: a `ScrollArea` on a surface, its ends fading into it | the `ScrollArea`'s, and `.stickyHeader(view)`, `.stickyFooter(view)`, `.fadeHeight(space)`, `.fadeBottom(false)`, `.fade(color)` (its `fadeColor`), `.matchSurfacePaint()`, `.style('primary' \| 'secondary' \| 'full')` (its `surface`), `.label` |
| `AutoHideBar(bar, ...body)` | `AutoHideBar`: the bar over the body, a strip while `collapsed` that peeks the bar under the pointer | `.collapsed()`, `.onReveal(fn)`, `.summary(view \| text)`, `.peek(false)`, `.barHeight(space)`, `.collapsedHeight(space)`, `.band(color)` (its `background`), `.peekDelay(duration)`, `.unpeekDelay(duration)`, `.hideMotion(motion)` and `.showMotion(motion)` (its `motion`), `.label` (the strip's button), `.testID` |
| `ResizeHandle(onResize)` | `ResizeHandle`: the separator a drag (or, on the web, an arrow key) resizes; `onResize` is told the distance from where the drag started | `.axis` (its `orientation`), `.style('grip' \| 'pill')`, `.thickness(space)`, `.onResizeStart`, `.onResizeEnd`, `.onNudge`, `.label`, `.testID` |

A `ScrollArea`'s and a `ScrollSurface`'s box modifiers are a view around the
scroll, as a `ScrollView`'s are; their margins and gap are the same code as
its. A bar, a handle, a toolbar and a sticky section take only their own
modifiers: a handle is placed by its container (an `.overlay(view, alignment)`
at a pane's edge). An `AutoHideBar`'s band is the surface it sits on unless
`.band()` names a colour, and a `ScrollSurface`'s fade is the surface's or,
with `.matchSurfacePaint()`, the nearest material's unless `.fade()` names
one: each a colour role (at an opacity too) painted from the theme, or an
app's own colour from `defineTokens({ color })`, never a raw value. A bar's
waits are duration tokens (Bloom's are `fast` before it peeks and `'250'`
before it collapses again) and its motions motion tokens (Bloom's own
ease-outs, 150ms collapsing and 200ms coming back, with none named). The edge
and scroll-metric providers are hooks and providers an app's own chrome
reads, not views.

## Image

`Image(source, alt?)` is Bloom's `BloomImage`: expo-image on a device,
react-native's `Image` on the web. `source` is anything `BloomImage` takes: a URL,
a bundled asset (`require()`) or a URI descriptor with headers.

```ts
Image(post.cover, 'Cover')
  .frame({ height: '5xl' })
  .aspectRatio(16 / 9)
  .contentFit('cover')
  .corner('lg')
  .crossFade('fast')
```

- **Box.** An image has no size until it loads, so it is sized like a view:
  `frame`, or one side and `aspectRatio` (width over height). The innermost
  layer is the image's own style, so `corner` rounds the pixels
  (`.clipped()` clips the box too) and
  `background` shows until they arrive.
- **Options.** `contentFit` takes `BloomImage`'s vocabulary (`cover`, the
  default, `contain`, `fill`, `none`, `scale-down`). `tint(role)` paints every
  opaque pixel one colour role, from the theme like an icon's fill.
  `crossFade` is a Bloom duration (`instant` 100, `fast` 150, `normal` 200,
  `slow` 300 ms); without it the image appears at once. `onLoad` receives the
  intrinsic `{ width, height }` and `onError` `{ error }`.
- **Name.** `alt` is the image's name, Bloom's `alt`: expo-image and React
  Native's Image read it as `accessibilityLabel` (natively it also makes the
  image an accessibility element), and on the web react-native-web's Image
  writes it as its `aria-label` and `<img alt>`, as React Native's Image with
  an `accessibilityLabel` does (`parity/image-name.cases.tsx`). `.label()`
  names it the same way on the web.
- **Presses.** `BloomImage` takes accessibility props but no presses, so an image
  with `onPress`, `onHover` or `hoverGroup` is drawn inside its pressable host,
  which also takes `grow` and `shrink`.

## Containers

Bloom's cards, rows, grouped sections, chips and notices, each the Bloom
component an app writes today (`src/controls/containers.tsx`). A selection is a
binding (`[value, setValue]`, what `useState` returns).

```ts
Card(
  CardHeader(CardTitle('Storage'), CardDescription('3 of 5 GB used')),
  CardBody(StorageBar({ used })),
  CardFooter(Button(Text('Manage')).onPress(manage)),
).style('outline').radius('lg')

GroupedSection(
  GroupedSectionItem('Wi-Fi').value(network).chevron().onPress(openWifi),
  GroupedSectionItem('Notifications').trailing(Toggle(notify)),
).title('Network').caption('Changes apply at once.')

Row('Ana Lima').subtitle('Online').leadingView(Avatar(ana.avatar)).onPress(open)
Chip(Icon(RiFilter3Line), Text('Unread')).toggle(unread)
Tabs(useState('posts'), TabItem('posts', 'Posts'), TabItem('replies', 'Replies').count(3))
EmptyState('No messages').icon(RiInbox2Line).action(Button(Text('Compose')).onPress(compose))
Admonition(Text('Sync is paused.'), Button(Text('Resume')).onPress(resume)).style('warning')
Kbd('⌘K')
ErrorBoundary(Feed()).title('The feed failed').resetKey(userId)
```

| Node | Draws |
| --- | --- |
| `Card(...)`, `CardHeader` / `CardBody` / `CardFooter(...)`, `CardTitle` / `CardDescription(text)` | Bloom `Card` and its parts; a card lays its children out as a stack does, `.axis()`, `.spacing()`, `.align()`, `.justify()`, in the view Bloom's Card holds them in (its `style`) |
| `Row(title?)` | Bloom `Item` (the list row; `Item` is the menu's row); `.onPress(undefined)` (a press chosen at render, `full ? undefined : add`) is an `Item` with no press, no button, as Bloom draws one |
| `GroupedSection(...rows)`, `GroupedSectionItem(title)` | Bloom `GroupedSection`, `GroupedSectionItem` |
| `GroupedSectionIcon(icon)`, `GroupedSectionDivider()`, `GroupedSectionCollapse(open, ...rows)` | Bloom `GroupedSectionIcon`, `GroupedSectionDivider`, `GroupedSectionCollapse` |
| `Chip(...content)` | Bloom `Chip`: the `Text` is its label, an `Icon` before or after it its glyphs; a glyph with a size or ink of its own (`Icon(X).size('sm').foreground('primary')`), or any other view, before or after the label is drawn as it is (Bloom's `leading` and `trailing`) |
| `ChipRow(...chips)`, `CappedChipRow(...chips)` | Bloom `ChipRow` (scrolls sideways), `CappedChipRow` (wraps, the rest behind "+N") |
| `EmptyState(title?, ...content)` | Bloom `EmptyState` (the content between its explanation and its actions) |
| `SelectionCard(title, ...body)` | Bloom `SelectionCard` |
| `Admonition(...content)` | Bloom's `AdmonitionRoot`, row, icon and content: a `Text` is `AdmonitionText`, a `Button` an action |
| `Tabs(selection, ...TabItem(value, label), ...TabContent(value, ...))` | Bloom `Tabs`, its `TabsTrigger`s and its `TabsContent`s; `.pressOpenTab('report' \| 'reselect')` is what pressing the open tab does (`reselect`: its content back to the top) |
| `Kbd(keys)` | Bloom `Kbd` |
| `ErrorBoundary(...content)` | Bloom `ErrorBoundary` |

- **Card.** `.style()` and `.tint()` are its appearance and tone, `.radius()`
  its corner: a corner token of Bloom's radius scale (`none` `2xs` `xs` `sm`
  `md` `lg` `xl` `2xl` `3xl` `full`, drawn as Bloom's `radius-0` …
  `radius-max` of the same value), `panel` or `concentric`,
  `.elevation('none' | 's' | 'm' | 'floating')`, `.outline('none' |
  'hairline' | 'thin' | 'medium')` its own stroke, `.clipped()` clips the
  content to the corner, `.cornerCurve('round' | 'smooth')` is its own curve
  (Bloom's `cornerCurve`, beside its radius). `.onPress` is the card's own press (`.disabled()`,
  `.label()`, `.role('link')`). `CardTitle` and `CardDescription` take
  `.lines(n)`. The box modifiers (padding, frame, background, corner, margin,
  flex) are the card's `style`, as an app writes them; on the web a role in
  it is its CSS variable, as Bloom's prepainted theme hands it to an app.
  The card's own box is the one its padding, frame and fill share, in that
  order: a `.background()` there is Bloom Card's `style.backgroundColor`,
  which its surface paints from, so `.background('transparent')` draws no
  surface at all (Mention's composer zeroes a card's fill so:
  `.padding('lg').frame({ width: 'full', height: 'full' }).background('transparent').grow()`).
  A background after a `.corner()` or a second padding is a view around the
  card, as anywhere (see [Order means nesting](#order-means-nesting)), and
  the card still paints its own surface inside it. Its corner and outline are
  `.radius()` and `.outline()`: a border width or a corner in its `style` is
  Bloom's to ignore, so the chain writes neither.
- **Row.** `.subtitle()`, `.leadingView(...)` and `.trailing(...)` (its slots,
  as views), `.size()`, `.lines(n)` (the title's), `.disabled()`,
  `.destructive()`, `.selected()`, `.active()`, `.highlighted()`,
  `.density('comfortable' | 'compact')` (its `contentDensity`), and its own
  press, hover and name.
- **Grouped sections.** `.title()`, `.caption()` (Bloom's `footer`: the name
  `footer` is the screen's), `.style('filled' | 'plain')`, `.separator('space'
  | 'line' | 'none')`, `.radius()`, `.density()` (reaching its rows). A row
  takes `.leadingView()` (its icon: a `GroupedSectionIcon(X).tint(tone)`, a
  glyph on a disc in one of Bloom's accent tones), `.description()`,
  `.value()`, `.trailing()` (a control), `.chevron()`, `.lines(n)`,
  `.wrapControl()` (a wide control wraps below the title, Bloom's
  `wrapControl`), `.descriptionLines(n)` (the description's lines, 2 by
  default; `0` never truncates), `.destructive()`, `.disabled()`, its own
  press and name, and the box
  modifiers as its own style. `GroupedSectionDivider().inset(space)` is a line
  between rows; `GroupedSectionCollapse(open, ...rows)` reveals and hides rows
  in place.
- **Chip.** `.style()`, `.tint()` (`undefined` is no tone of its own, so `.tint(sensitive ? 'danger' : undefined)` paints only while it holds), `.size('xs'…'2xl')`, `.hue()`,
  `.toggle(binding)` (a filter chip: checked through its binding),
  `.selected()`, `.onClose(remove, label?)`, `.disabled()`, `.onPress()`,
  `.role('checkbox' | 'radio' | 'tab')` (what it is to assistive technology).
  `.style('inverted')` is Bloom's inverted chip; its `color` is `.tint()`.
- **Chip rows.** `ChipRow(...)` takes `.spacing()`, `.contentInset()`,
  `.role('radiogroup' | 'tablist')`, `.label()`, `.disabled()`, `.fade(color)`
  (the colour its web edges fade into: a role painted from the theme, or an
  app's own colour), `.ringInset()`
  (room for a chip's focus ring) and `.keyboardTaps('always' | 'never' |
  'handled')` (whether a tap reaches a chip while the keyboard is open);
  `CappedChipRow(...)` takes `.lines(n)`, `.spacing()`, `.role('list')` and
  `.label()`.
- **EmptyState.** `.icon()`, `.description()`, `.media('glyph' | 'circle')`,
  `.style('comfortable' | 'compact')`; `.action(Button(...))` and
  `.secondaryAction(Button(...))` are its buttons, read from the `Button` node:
  its label, leading glyph, press (a function, or a `navigate()` through the
  screen's navigator), `.disabled()`, `.loading()`, `.label()` and `.testID()`.
  `.suggestions(...buttons)` are its chips (each `Button`'s `.id()`, label,
  glyph and press), `.suggestionsLabel()` their name, `.illustration(view)` a
  mark in place of the icon, `.caption(view)` the note under the actions, and
  `.frame({ minHeight })` the floor that holds a band open.
- **Admonition.** `.style('info' | 'tip' | 'warning' | 'error' |
  'apology')`, `.size()`. One `Text` draws what Bloom's `<Admonition>` draws.
- **Tabs.** `.style('underline' | 'pill' | 'filled')`, `.fullWidth()`,
  `.label()` (the strip's name); a `TabItem` takes `.icon()`, `.count()`,
  `.disabled()`, `.testID()`, `.iconOnly()`, `.reorderable()`, `.trailing()`
  (a control in its chrome, not its press) and `.onLongPress()`, and its own
  box and label over Bloom's: `.frame()` and `.padding()` are `TabsTrigger`'s
  `style`, `.font()`, `.leading()` and `.weight()` its `textStyle` (Mention's
  home tabs: `.frame({ height: '38', minWidth: '76' }).padding('left', 'md')
  .padding('right', 'md').padding('vertical', 'none').font('md').leading('18px')
  .weight(open ? 'bold' : 'medium')`, the 38pt strip and 76pt tab the space
  tokens `'38'` and `'76'`); the strip's
  `.onReorder(from, to)` keeps a new order and `.dragTranslation(value)` is a
  swipe's travel the underline follows. What each tab shows is
  `TabContent(value, ...)` inside the strip (Bloom's `TabsContent`), or a
  conditional in the screen (`tab === 'posts' ? Posts() : Replies()`).
- **SelectionCard.** `.toggle(binding)` makes it a checkbox, one of several;
  `.selected(on)` with `.onPress()` a radio, the screen choosing. It takes
  `.description()`, `.icon()`, `.indicator('check' | 'radio')`,
  `.illustration(view)` (its media), `.density('compact')`, `.disabled()`,
  `.invalid()`, `.nativeID(id)` and `.describedBy(id)` (its description's id),
  and its body as content.
- **Kbd** takes `.size('sm' | 'md')`. **ErrorBoundary** takes `.title()`,
  `.message()`, `.retryLabel()`, `.resetKey()`, `.onError()` and
  `.fallback(view)` or `.fallback(({ error, retry }) => view)`, and the box
  modifiers: Bloom's boundary then draws one view of that style around its
  content (and its fallback), and none without one.

Not in the language: the class recipes (`className` and its kin), which the
className table maps.

## Generic components

The Bloom components apps compose beyond the primitives and the controls, each
drawn as the component itself: a node maps its modifiers onto the props an app
writes today, so it looks and behaves as that component does (proven by the
parity suite, family by family). An input takes a `Binding` (`[value, set]`,
what `useState` returns), so the screen owns the value. The box modifiers
(`padding`, `frame`, `background`…) reach the component's `style`, `.label()`
its accessible name and `.testID()` its test id.

### Date and time pickers

```ts
const [day, setDay] = useState<Date | null>(null);
DatePicker([day, setDay]).bounds(today, null).placeholder('Check-in').label('Check-in')
DateRangePicker([range, setRange]).quickSelect(false).presented([open, setOpen])
Calendar([day, setDay]).weekStart(1).unavailable(isWeekend)
RangeCalendar([range, setRange]).months(2)
TimeField([time, setTime]).bounds('09:00', '18:00').step(15).hourFormat('12h').size('sm')
```

Bloom's `DatePicker`, `DateRangePicker`, `Calendar`, `RangeCalendar` and
`TimeField`. Every calendar and picker takes `.bounds(min, max?)` (`null`
leaves an end open), `.unavailable(fn)`, `.weekStart(0…6)`, `.locale(tag)` and
`.labels({...})` (wording of your own; Bloom already speaks its strings in the
locale). A picker adds `.placeholder()`, `.disabled()` and
`.presented([open, setOpen])` (its popup, controlled); a range picker
`.quickSelect(false)`. A calendar takes `.initialMonth(date)`, a range calendar
`.months(1 | 2)`. A `TimeField` holds a 24h `"HH:mm"` string: `.bounds()` in
that form, `.step(minutes)`, `.hourFormat('12h' | '24h')` (what is drawn; the
value stays 24h), `.size('sm' | 'md')`, `.placeholder()` and `.disabled()`.

```ts
MeetingScheduler([booking, setBooking], host, meeting, 'Amsterdam')
  .timeSlots(['09:00', '09:30', '10:00']).bounds(today, null).hourFormat('12h')
```

Bloom's `MeetingScheduler`: a trigger that opens the host's card, a month and
the chosen day's time slots. `MeetingScheduler(booking, host, meeting,
timezone)`: the binding holds the booking (a day and a 24h time, or `null`),
set by its "Send meeting"; `host` and `meeting` are Bloom's card data. It
takes the calendars' `.bounds()`, `.unavailable()`, `.weekStart()`,
`.locale()` and `.labels()`, and `.timeSlots([...])`, `.hourFormat()` (how its
times are drawn at first; the reader can switch), `.onTimezonePress()`,
`.triggerTitle()`, `.disabled()` and `.presented([open, setOpen])`.

### Ratings

```ts
Rating(place.rating).count(place.reviews).size('sm')
Rating(4.3).style('stars').starTint('warning').countTint('muted')
RatingInput([score, setScore]).size('lg').label('Overall rating')
RatingBar('Cleanliness', 4.9).valueLabel('4.9')
RatingSummary(average, count, distribution)
```

Bloom's `Rating`, `RatingInput`, `RatingBar` and `RatingSummary`. `Rating(value)`
takes a number or a string drawn as given (`null` draws `New`): `.count()`,
`.countStyle('parenthesis' | 'reviews')`, `.size('sm' | 'md')`,
`.style('compact' | 'stars')`, `.showsValue(false)`, `.starSize(space)`,
`.fillValue(n)` (what the stars fill to, apart from a localised string),
`.labels({ reviews, new })`, and its colours as roles: `.tint()` (stars and
value, over a brand colour), `.starTint()`, `.countTint()`, `.emptyTint()`.
`RatingInput([value, set])` takes `.max()`, `.size('sm' | 'md' | 'lg')`,
`.disabled()` and `.starLabel(fn)`; name it with `.label()`. `RatingBar(label,
value)` takes `.max()`, `.valueLabel()`, `.labelWidth(space)`, and `.reveal()`
and `.revealMotion()` as a meter's bar does; `RatingSummary(average, count, distribution)` takes `.labels({ count, outOf,
noRatings })`.

### Meters and progress bars

```ts
Meter(3, 5).valueText('3 of 5').label('Steps')
ProgressBar(sent, total).tint('success').label('Upload')
ProgressBar(0).indeterminate().label('Transcoding')
MeterRing(score, 10).size('80').content(Text(String(score))).label('Match')
ProgressRing(progress).onStop(cancel, 'Stop downloading').label('Download')
StatBar('Points', 720, 1000).rangeLabels('0', '1,000').trailing(Icon(RiTrophyLine).size('sm'))
SplitBar('Inflow', 62, '$1.2k', '$740')
```

Bloom's `Meter`, `MeterRing` and `StatBar`. `Meter(value, total?)` and
`MeterRing(value, total?)` are a measurement (a score, a charge: ARIA's
`meter`); `ProgressBar` and `ProgressRing` are the same components as a task's
progress (`progressbar`). Name each with `.label()`. Both take
`.valueText()`, `.indeterminate()`, `.decorative()` (no role, hidden: the
drawing inside a control that names itself), `.tint()` and `.trackTint()`
(roles), `.thickness(space)` and `.fillDuration(duration)` (the web's ease to a
new value). A bar is the width of its parent unless framed, and takes
`.barCorner(corner)` and its entrance: `.reveal({ visible, once?, delay? })`
(it fills from empty once `visible`; `once` keeps it filled after it is first
seen; `delay` a duration token) and `.revealMotion(motion)`, how it fills (a
timed motion token: one of Bloom's motions with a duration, or an app's own
from `defineTokens({ motion })`; Bloom's 300ms ease by default). A ring takes
`.size(space)`, `.backdrop(role)`, `.cap('round' | 'butt')`, `.content(view)`,
`.onStop(action, label)` and, decorative, `.progress(sharedValue)`.
`StatBar(label, value, max)` takes `.rangeLabels(min, max)`, `.trailing(view)`,
`.tint()`, `.trackTint()` and `.thickness()`; `SplitBar(label, percent, left,
right)` is its split.

### Stickers

`Lottie(url)` is Bloom's `Sticker` (see [Animations](#animations)): it plays
once unless `.loop()`, `.paused(bool)` holds its frame, `.fallback(Image(still,
label))` is the still it shows while loading, where it cannot play and under
reduced motion (its label names the sticker), a frame's width alone is its
edge, and `.decorative()` hides it from assistive technology when its container
names it. A sticker with no animation is its still: an `Image`.

### Tables

```ts
Table(
  TableHeader(
    TableColumn('Name').width('160').sort(direction, toggleSort),
    TableColumn('Amount').share(2).align('end'),
  ),
  TableBody(
    ...invoices.map((invoice) =>
      TableRow(TableCell(invoice.name), TableCell(invoice.total)).selected(invoice.picked).id(invoice.id),
    ),
  ).emptyState('No invoices yet'),
).size('sm').minWidth('640').label('Invoices')
```

Bloom's `Table`, `TableHeader`, `TableColumn`, `TableBody`, `TableRow` and
`TableCell`, drawn in place so the table reads its columns' layout as Bloom's
does. `.size('sm' | 'md')` is the row density, `.minWidth(space)` the width
below which the table scrolls sideways. A column's label and a cell's content
are a string or number (drawn in the table's own type) or a view. A column and
a cell take `.width(space | 'fit')` (`fit` hugs the content), `.share(n)` of
the remaining width, `.minWidth()` and `.align('start' | 'center' | 'end')`; a
cell follows its column unless it sets its own. A column takes
`.sort(direction, action)` (`ascending`, `descending`, or `none`, which keeps
the chevron's room) and `.prefix(view)` (a select-all checkbox, outside the
sort button). A body takes `.emptyState()`, a row `.selected(bool)`. The
header's columns are read as Bloom's `TableColumn` elements: give them their
layout with these modifiers, not a box of their own.

### Rows and lists

```ts
ListItem('Ana Lima').supporting('Design lead').prefix(Avatar(ana.avatar)).onPress(open)
ConversationRow(chat).active(chat.id === openId).swipeActions({ right: archive })
List(entries).separator('inset').emptyState(Text('Nobody yet'))
VirtualList(messages, messageRow).itemKey(byId).estimatedItemSize(72).onEndReached(loadMore).screen()
ListSelectionBar(selected.length, total).selecting(true).rowActions(bulk, run)
```

Bloom's `ListItem`, `List`, `VirtualList` and `ListSelectionBar`.
`ListItem(title?)` is the one row: a title (a string in the row's type, or a
view), `.supporting()` and `.supportingLines(1 | 2 | 3 | 'adaptive')`, the
slots `.prefix()` (what leads it), `.trailing()`, `.content()` (a body of its
own), `.footnote()` (under the text) and `.attachments()`; `.density('comfortable' | 'compact')`
and `.compactPadding()`, `.active()`, `.disabled()`, `.link(href)`,
`.onPrefetch()`, its press and long press, `.label()` (its full name);
selection with `.checkable([checked, set])`, `.selecting()` and
`.selectionLabel()`; `.rowActions(actions, onAction)`, `.swipeActions(actions,
full?)`, `.swipeEnabled()` and `.contextMenu(view)`. `PersonRow`, `CallRow`,
`ConversationRow` and `MailRow` are its recipes, over Bloom's data. A mail
row's star is `.starred([starred, setStarred])`, a binding drawn into its data
(Bloom's `starred` and `onStarredChange`; bound, the star is a button), so
`MailRow` is a node of its own (`mailRow`) with the row's modifiers and that
one; a `List`'s entries stay Bloom's data, each entry's star with it.

`List(entries)` and `SectionedList(sections)` take Bloom's `ListEntry` data:
`.separator('none' | 'line' | 'inset')`, `.scrollable()`, `.scrollHeight()`,
`.stickyHeaders()`, `.sectionIndex()`, `.headerContent()`, `.footerContent()`,
`.emptyState()`, `.loading()` and `.loadingRows(n)`.

`VirtualList(data, row)` is Bloom's: FlashList (or FlatList) on native, the
virtualizer on the web. `row({ item, index })` returns the item's view, or the
element FlashList's own `renderItem` returns: it is handed FlashList's argument
as Bloom passes it, so an app's named `renderPost` is the row as it is
(`VirtualList(posts, renderPost)`), its element drawn unchanged. A row that
returns a language root (`toElement(chain)`) is drawn as part of the list's
tree, so a row costs what its JSX does, with no boundary of its own. Write a
list of language rows this way rather than as Bloom's JSX `VirtualList` whose
`renderItem` returns `toElement(...)`: there each row is a boundary of its own
(a component and a context read per row). Keep it stable
(module scope, or a `useCallback`), and the list keeps its `renderItem`, so the
engine re-renders a row only when its item changes, as with JSX; a new arrow per
render would redraw every memoised row. It takes `.contentMargins(edge?, space)`, `.contentGrows(on?)` and
`.spacing(space)`, its `contentContainerStyle`'s padding and `gap`, as every
scroll of the language's takes them (`paddingHorizontal: 16, gap: 8` is
`.contentMargins('horizontal', 'lg').spacing('sm')`; the room for views pinned
at its edges adds to that padding), `.itemKey()`, `.itemType()` (FlashList's recycling type),
`.estimatedItemSize(px)`, `.columns()`, `.itemLayout(fn)` (Bloom's `overrideItemLayout`:
`fn(layout, item, index, columns)` sets `layout.span`, so a full-width row sits in a grid of
columns), `.onEndReached(action, threshold?)`,
`.refreshable([refreshing, setRefreshing])` or `.refreshable({ refreshing, onRefresh })`, `.stickyIndices()`
(each chosen at render with `undefined` for none, as JSX writes `onEndReached={hasMore ? loadMore : undefined}`,
`onRefresh={signedIn ? refetch : undefined}` and `stickyHeaderIndices={tabBar ? [0] : undefined}`: no end call, no
pull to refresh, no sticky row),
`.restorationKey()`, `.headerContent()`, `.headerFitsContent()` (the header is its content's
height and the list's width, `ListHeaderComponentStyle`'s `flexGrow: 0, flexShrink: 0,
alignSelf: 'stretch'`, so the engine never stretches it to the viewport and a sticky row after it
starts under it), `.footerContent()`, `.emptyState()`,
`.screen(options?)` (Bloom's `screen`, `{}` by default: the list is its screen's
scroller, binding the header's fade, the bottom bar's fold, the reselect and the
insets, without a `ScrollView` around it; it must be inside a screen),
`.scrollbar('scroll' | 'hover' | 'always' | 'never')` (the native indicator; the
web's bar is the screen's scroller's), `.keyboardTaps()` and
`.scrollDismissesKeyboard()` (`keyboardShouldPersistTaps` and
`keyboardDismissMode`, as a `ScrollView`'s), and `.tuning({...})`: the engine's
own knobs, each as Bloom takes it (`overscan`, the web's rows beyond the
viewport; `removeClippedSubviews`, `maxToRenderPerBatch`, `windowSize`,
`initialNumToRender`, `recycleItems`, `maintainVisibleContentPosition`,
`drawDistance`, `maxItemsInRecyclePool`). `ListSelectionBar(count, total)`
takes `.selecting()`, `.onSelectAll()`, `.onClear()`, `.rowActions()` and
`.labels()`.

### Carousels

```ts
Carousel(...photos.map((photo) => CarouselItem(Image(photo.url, photo.alt)).id(photo.id)))
  .arrows('header').headerContent(Text('Photos').font('headline')).dots().label('Photos')
Carousel(...cards).align('center').loop().groupSize(2).page([index, setIndex]).label('Plans')
CarouselDots(count, [index, setIndex]).style('onMedia').max(5)
```

Bloom's `Carousel`, `CarouselItem` and `CarouselDots`. A carousel is named
with `.label()`; it takes `.headerContent()` and `.footerContent()`, `.loop()`,
`.groupSize(n)` (slides an arrow moves), `.arrows('header' | 'overlay' |
'footer' | false)`, `.arrowsVisibility('always' | 'hover')`, `.arrowsInset()`,
`.hidesUnavailableArrows()`, `.dots()`, `.align('start' | 'center')`,
`.spacing()` (between slides), `.contentInset()`, `.page([index, set])` and
`.labels({ previous, next, dot })`. A slide is the carousel's width unless it
sets `.width(space)`. `CarouselDots(count, [index, set])` takes
`.shape('pill' | 'dot')`, `.style('default' | 'onMedia')`, `.max(n)` and
`.labels({ dot })`.

### Markdown

```ts
Markdown(reply.text).streaming(!reply.done).size('sm')
Markdown(notes).onLinkPress(openLink).codeBlock((code, language) => CodeBlock(code, language))
```

Bloom's `Markdown`: headings, lists, task lists, quotes, tables, links, rules
and code, in Bloom's type ramp. `.streaming()` draws text that is still
arriving (an open span or block as it will close), `.size('sm' | 'md' | 'lg')`
sets its ramp, `.onLinkPress(fn)` replaces opening a link, and
`.codeBlock((code, language) => view)` draws a code block of your own.

```ts
Text(InlineMarkdown(reply.summary).streaming(!reply.done))
Text(InlineMarkdown(post.body).plainRun(mentions).suffix(Text(' Read more').link(url)))
```

Inline markdown inside a line is `InlineMarkdown(source)`, Bloom's own: a run
of a `Text` (emphasis, strikethrough, code and links as spans of it), its
source a string or Bloom's parsed runs. It takes `.streaming()`,
`.onLinkPress()`, `.plainRun(text => runs)` (each plain run, not inside links
or code), `.linkView(link => runs)` (a link of your own; `undefined` keeps
Bloom's) and `.suffix(runs)`. Each hands back runs of the text, as `Text`
takes them: one, or several in order, strings beside spans and element runs,
so a post's linkified plain run is its prose as strings and its mentions,
tags and URLs as spans, with no span around the prose (see [Text](#text)).

### Sliders

```ts
Slider([volume, setVolume], 'Volume').onCommit(save)
Slider([radius, setRadius]).bounds(1, 50).step(1).valueFormat((km) => `${km} km`).label('Radius')
RangeSlider([price, setPrice], 'Price').bounds(0, 500).step(10).labels({ lower: 'From', upper: 'To' })
```

Bloom's `Slider` and `RangeSlider`. The binding is the value as it slides
(`onValueChange`); `.onCommit(fn)` is called once where the thumb comes to
rest (`onSlidingComplete`). A title is its visible label and name; without one,
name it with `.label()`. Both take `.size()`, `.tint(tone)`, `.bounds(min,
max)`, `.step()`, `.keyboardStep()`, `.disabled()`, `.thickness()` (the
track), `.thumbSize()`, `.fillTint()`, `.trackTint()` and `.thumbTint()`
(roles), `.showsValue(false)` (no bubble) and `.valueFormat(fn)`; a range
slider names its thumbs with `.labels({ lower, upper })`.

### Accordions

```ts
Accordion(
  [open, setOpen],
  AccordionItem('shipping', AccordionTrigger('Shipping'), AccordionContent(Text(shipping))),
  AccordionItem('returns', AccordionTrigger('Returns'), AccordionContent(Text(returns))).disabled(),
).allowsMultiple()
```

Bloom's `Accordion`, `AccordionItem`, `AccordionTrigger` and
`AccordionContent`, fully controlled: the binding holds the open item's value
(or the values, with `.allowsMultiple()`, or `undefined`). An accordion takes
`.size()` and `.revealMotion(motion)`: a section opens on Bloom's spring, or on
a timed motion token in its place (one of Bloom's motions with a duration, or
an app's own); an item
`.disabled()`; a trigger's title is a string in its type or a view, and
`.indicator(view)` replaces its chevron.

### Code

```ts
Code('bun run build')
Pre(output).lineNumbers()
CodeLines(patch).language('ts').highlight(['+2', '-3'])
CodeBlock(snippet).language('tsx').filename('row.tsx').diff(3, 1)
```

Bloom's `Code` (inline monospace), `Pre` (preformatted text in its well; its
box is the well), `CodeLines` and `CodeBlock`. Each but `Code` takes
`.language()` (JavaScript and TypeScript are highlighted) and
`.lineNumbers()`; numbered lines and a card take `.highlight(['+2', '-3',
'4'])` and `.wrap()`, lines `.size('sm' | 'md')`, and a card
`.languageLabel()`, `.filename()`, `.diff(additions, deletions)`,
`.copyable(false)`, `.onCopy()`, `.headerContent(view)` and `.labels({ copy,
copied })`.

### Steppers

```ts
Stepper([guests, setGuests]).bounds(1, 16).label('Guests')
StepperRow([children, setChildren], 'Children').supporting('Ages 2 to 12').dividers()
```

Bloom's `Stepper` and `StepperRow`: the binding's count, `.bounds(min,
max)`, `.step()`, `.disabled()`, `.size('sm' | 'md')`, `.style('separate' |
'outline')`, `.valueFormat(fn)`, `.onRemove(fn)` (at the lowest count, − becomes
remove) and `.labels({ decrement, increment, remove })`. A row's title is a
string or a view; it takes `.supporting()` and `.dividers()`.

### Tooltips

```ts
Tooltip(Button(Icon(RiInformationLine)).shape('circle').label('About'), 'About this page')
  .presented([hinting, setHinting]).side('top')
```

Bloom's `Tooltip`, controlled, because what opens it differs by platform (a
resting pointer, a long press). `Tooltip(trigger, content)`: the content is a
string (also its accessible text) or a view named with `.label()`. It takes
`.presented([visible, set])`, `.side('top' | 'bottom')`, `.openDelay(duration)`
(a duration token; a desktop waits `'1000'` by default, `'0'` opens at once) and
`.size('sm' | 'md')`; the trigger carries its own box.

### Breadcrumbs

```ts
Breadcrumb(
  BreadcrumbItem('Home').link('/'),
  BreadcrumbItem('Projects').onPress(openProjects),
  BreadcrumbItem(project.name).active(true),
).collapses().label('Breadcrumb')
```

Bloom's `Breadcrumb` and `BreadcrumbItem`. A trail takes `.size()`,
`.collapses()` (its middle folds into a menu; it scrolls by default),
`.separatorView(view)` and `.labels({ overflow })`. An item's title is a string
or a view; it takes `.size()`, `.icon()`, `.prefix(view)`, `.link(href)`, its
press, `.active(true)` (the current page), `.disabled()` and `.hoverMenu()`.

### Activity heatmaps

```ts
ActivityHeatmap(days).days(182).weekStart(1).onPressDay(showDay)
ActivityHeatmap(days).levels([2, 5]).colorScale(['success/20', 'success/half', 'success'])
```

Bloom's `ActivityHeatmap`: `.days(n)`, `.endDate()`, `.levels([...])`,
`.emptyTint(role)`, `.cellSize()`, `.spacing()`, `.weekStart(0 | 1)`,
`.labels({ months, weekdays })` and `.onPressDay()`. Its steps are the accent's
unless `.colorScale([...roles])` names them, faintest to strongest
(`.levels()` + 1 of them; a role at an opacity included).

### Tag fields

```ts
TagField([topics, setTopics], 'Topics').tagSuggestions(known, 5).max(10).placeholder('Add a topic')
```

Bloom's `TagField`: the binding's tags; a title is its visible label and name.
It takes `.placeholder()`, `.tagSuggestions(list, max?)`, `.allowsCreate(false)`,
`.max(n)` and `.onMaxReached()`, `.normalize(fn)`, `.query([text, set])` (the
typed text, when the screen owns it), `.tint(accentTone)`, `.size()`,
`.disabled()`, `.invalid()` and `.labels()`.

### File uploads

```ts
FileUpload().allowedExtensions(['pdf']).maxBytes(5_000_000).onFileSelected(upload)
  .file(current).uploadProgress(progress)
```

Bloom's `FileUpload`: `.file()` and `.uploadProgress(0…100)` (the screen's
upload), `.onFileSelected()`, `.onUploadComplete()`, `.onReject()`,
`.picker(fn)`, `.allowedExtensions()`, `.maxBytes()`, `.fileIcon(file =>
view)`, `.disabled()` and `.labels()`.

### Videos

```ts
Video(player).contentFit('cover').frame({ width: 'full', height: '200' })
```

Bloom's `VideoView` (expo-video's, inline and on Android's texture view by
default): the app's own player, `.contentFit()`, `.nativeControls()` and
`.allowsPictureInPicture()`.

### One-time codes

```ts
InputOtp([code, setCode]).length(6).groupSize(3).onComplete(verify).label('Verification code')
```

Bloom's `InputOtp`: one box per character, the binding's code (cleaned of what
the boxes do not take). It takes `.length(n)` (6 by default),
`.characters('numeric' | 'alphanumeric')`, `.groupSize(n)` (a gap after every
`n` boxes), `.onComplete(code => …)`, `.invalid()`, `.disabled()` and
`.autoFocus()`; `.label()` names the group.

### Colour swatch pickers

```ts
ColorSwatchPicker('Brand colour', [brand, setBrand], BRAND_COLORS).labels(colorNames)
```

Bloom's `ColorSwatchPicker`: a wrapping row of swatches, one chosen.
`ColorSwatchPicker(label, binding, colors)`: the label names the whole, each
colour (a CSS colour) is its own value. It takes `.labels({ [color]: name })`
(what a screen reader says of a swatch; its colour string otherwise),
`.size(space)` (a swatch's diameter, `4xl` by default) and `.disabled()`.

### Animated checks

```ts
AnimatedCheck(status).size('64')
```

Bloom's `AnimatedCheck`: a ring and a check that draw themselves as the view
appears, and again whenever its trigger (`AnimatedCheck(trigger)`) changes, as
an app plays it through its ref. It takes `.size(space)` (`2xl` by default) and
`.tint(colour)` (`success` by default); its box is a view around it.

### Choice lists

```ts
ChoiceList('Accounts', [chosen, choose], accounts, (account, { selected }) =>
  VStack(Avatar(account.label), Text(account.label).weight(selected ? 'semibold' : 'regular')),
).spacing('28').optionBox({ padding: { horizontal: '6', vertical: 'xs' }, corner: 'md' })
```

Bloom's `ChoiceList`: a few choices, one chosen, one keyboard stop (the
arrows move, a press or Space chooses). `ChoiceList(label, binding, options,
content)`: the label names the whole, each option (Bloom's `{ value, label,
disabled?, testID? }`) is named by its label, and `content(option, { selected,
disabled })` draws it, decoratively. It takes `.axis('horizontal' |
'vertical')`, `.spacing()` (`lg` by default), `.optionBox({ padding, corner,
align })` (each option's padding by edge, as `.padding(edge, token)` names
them, its corner and how it aligns its content) and `.disabled()`.

### Context menus

```ts
ContextMenu(
  Text(message.body).padding('md'),
  Item('Copy').icon(RiFileCopyLine).tag('copy'),
  Divider(),
  Item('Delete').icon(RiDeleteBinLine).destructive().tag('remove'),
).actions(MessageActions({ message })).label('Message options')
```

Bloom's `ContextMenu` on a view: a long press opens it on native (on Android
and OxyOS, the lens menu of Bloom's lens engine, morphing out of the press),
the secondary button on the web. Its rows are a `Menu`'s (`Item`, `Submenu`,
`Section`, `Divider`, one `menu()` content) and `.actions()` resolves its
tagged rows. The view's box and `.label()` are the area's; `.disabled()` keeps
it shut, and `.presentedAt([anchor, setAnchor])` holds where it is open (a
point or a box, `null` while closed) when the screen owns it. Its rows are read
with the view, a `menu()` content's too: the lens menu takes them before the
press.

### Collapsibles

```ts
Collapsible(expanded, Text(order.notes)).revealMotion('exit').keepsMounted(false)
```

Bloom's `Collapsible`: a measured reveal of its children while `open` is true,
with no trigger of its own (the screen's row or button flips the value). It
takes `.revealMotion(motion)` (a timed motion token: one of Bloom's motions
with a duration, or an app's own from `defineTokens({ motion })`; Bloom's
300ms ease-in-out by default),
`.revealsOnAppear()` (mounted open, it opens from nothing) and
`.keepsMounted(false)` (the content unmounts once shut).

### Drop zones

```ts
DropZone(['files'], (event) => upload(event.data.files), Text('Drop photos here'))
  .fileTypes(['image/*']).dragActions(['copy', 'move'], 'copy').onDragEnter(highlight)
```

Bloom's `DropZone`: its views take a drop of the kinds named (`files`, `text`,
`url`, `html` or a media type) from another application or this one, and the
handler receives what was dropped. It takes `.fileTypes([...])` (the media
types of files it takes), `.dragActions(actions, preferred?)` (`copy`, `move`,
`link`), `.folder()` (where a native platform puts dropped files),
`.disabled()`, and `.onDragEnter()`, `.onDragOver()` and `.onDragLeave()`.
Where the platform has no drop target, it is a view that takes nothing.

### Image aspect ratios

```ts
const Photo = view(({ uri }: { uri: string }) => {
  const [ratio, setRatio] = useState(() => getAspectRatio(uri) ?? DEFAULT_ASPECT_RATIO);
  return Image(uri).frame({ width: 'full' }).aspectRatio(ratio).onLoad(({ width, height }) => {
    setAspectRatio(uri, width / height);
    setRatio(width / height);
  });
});
```

Bloom's `image-aspect-ratio-cache` is an API, not a view, so the language takes
it as it is (`@oxy.so/bloom/image-aspect-ratio-cache`): the one cache of image
shapes by URI that a feed's thumbnails and the gallery share. A view reads it
into `.aspectRatio()` and records what `.onLoad()` measures, so a list stops
jumping and the gallery opens at the right shape.

### Video playback

```ts
VideoPlayback(AppLayout(...))                       // once, at the root
VideoViewability(onScreen, ...posts.map((post) => VideoViewabilityScope(post.id, PostRow(post))))
```

Bloom's `video-playback`: one audible player app-wide and visibility-driven
autoplay. Its providers are views around what they hold, with no box of their
own: `VideoPlayback(...)` (`VideoPlaybackProvider`; `.autoplay(false)` overrides
the viewer's preference for a subtree), `VideoViewability(viewable, ...)` (a
list's keys on screen: a set derived while rendering, or the store
`createViewableKeysStore()` makes for its callbacks) and
`VideoViewabilityScope(key, ...)`. Its hooks (`useVideoPlayback`,
`useViewableVisible`, `useVideoPlaybackControl`) are used as they are inside a
`view()`.

### Media flights

```ts
MediaFlightLayer()                                   // once, at the root
MediaFlightHost(post.id, { uri: post.photo }).frame({ width: 'full', height: '200' }).contentFit('cover')
MediaSurface({ kind: 'video', player }).nativeControls(true).videoSlot(({ player, style }) => Embed(<MyVideo player={player} style={style} />))
```

Bloom's `media-flight`: media that flies between two places and survives the
route change. `MediaFlightLayer()` is where flights paint; `MediaFlightHost(id,
content)` is media a flight leaves from and lands in, known by its id;
`MediaSurface(content)`, `MediaPoster(content)` and `EmptyMediaSurface()` are
the surfaces it is drawn with. Content is an image (`{ uri, previewUri? }`) or
the app's own player (`{ kind: 'video', player, poster? }`). A surface takes
`.contentFit('contain' | 'cover')`, `.nativeControls()`, `.surfaceType()`
(Android's `textureView`, the default, or `surfaceView`), `.flightId()` and
`.videoSlot()` (the video drawn by a view of your own). The flights themselves
are Bloom's controller, `useMediaFlight()` (`measureAnchor`, `flyTo`,
`flyBack`, `handOff`, all by id), used as it is inside a `view()`.

### Avatar flights

```ts
const Thread = view(() => {
  const flights = useAvatarFlights();
  return ZStack(
    VStack(AvatarAnchor(flights, 'composer', Avatar(agent.image)), AvatarAnchor(flights, reply.id, Text(reply.text))),
    AvatarFlightLayer(flights),
  );
});
```

Bloom's `avatar-flight`: avatars that fly from one place on the screen to
another. `AvatarAnchor(flights, key, ...)` is a place they leave and land (the
view's box is its style; it hides while its avatar is in the air) and
`AvatarFlightLayer(flights)` where they fly. The flights are Bloom's hooks,
used as they are inside a `view()`: `useAvatarFlights()` (`launch`, `cancel`,
by anchor key), `useAvatarJoin()` and `centeredLanding`.

### Media galleries

```ts
const [viewing, setViewing] = useState(false);
const [page, setPage] = useState(0);
HStack(...post.media.map((media, i) =>
  MediaFlightHost(`${post.id}-${i}`, media).frame({ width: '120', height: '120' }).onPress(() => {
    setPage(i);
    setViewing(true);
  }),
)).fullScreenCover(
  [viewing, setViewing],
  Gallery(post.media).page([page, setPage]).sourceAnchors(post.media.map((_, i) => `${post.id}-${i}`)),
)
```

Bloom's `ZoomableMediaGallery`, presented by a binding as SwiftUI's
`.fullScreenCover(isPresented:)` presents: any view's
`.fullScreenCover([isPresented, set], Gallery(items))` draws the gallery beside
it. Setting the binding opens it at its page; clearing it closes it as its
backdrop does; the gallery closing itself clears it. `Gallery(items)` (images
and the app's own videos) is content, never placed in a stack. It takes
`.page([index, set])` (where it opens, and what swiping sets), `.style('overlay'
| 'page')`, `.pageIndicator('dots' | 'thumbnails')`, `.mediaCorner()` (`full` is
a circle at every size, as an avatar is), `.nativeControls()`,
`.videoOverlay(media => view)`, `.labels()` and `.sourceAnchors(ids)`: the
`MediaFlightHost` ids its items open from and fly back to, so no ref reaches
the screen (an item without one fades in and out at the centre).

### Media inset borders

```ts
ZStack(Image(photo).corner('lg'), MediaInsetBorder().mediaCorner('lg'))
```

Bloom's `MediaInsetBorder`: a theme hairline that fills its parent, drawn
inside a media edge so a white photo still has a boundary. `.opaque()` is the
full-contrast border (softened by default); `.mediaCorner()` follows the
media's corner (a view's `.corner()` clips, which the hairline must not).

### Fact strips

```ts
FactStrip(
  Fact('1.2K ratings', '4.7').caption(Rating(4.7).style('stars').showsValue(false)),
  Fact('Category', 'Productivity').caption('Work').onPress(openCategory),
).contentInset('lg').label('About this app')
```

Bloom's `FactStrip`: its facts side by side with a hairline between them,
scrolling sideways when they do not fit. `Fact(label, value)` is one fact (a
string value is drawn large, or a view); it takes `.caption()` (a string drawn
small, or a view), `.onPress()`, `.label()` (its one accessible name) and
`.id()` (its label by default), and no box. The strip takes
`.minItemWidth(space)` (`96` by default) and `.contentInset(space)`.

### Photo grids

```ts
SortablePhotoGrid([photos, setPhotos]).onRemove(remove).onRetry(retry).onAdd(pick).max(10)
```

Bloom's `SortablePhotoGrid`: the binding's photos (Bloom's `SortablePhoto`:
an id, a uri, and an upload's status and progress), the first one the cover; a
drag on the web or a move button writes the new order. It takes
`.onRemove(id => …)`, `.onRetry(id => …)`, `.onAdd()` (the add tile),
`.max(n)`, `.addHint()`, `.columns(n)`, `.imageVariant()` (the image
resolver's variant each photo is drawn at), `.disabled()` and `.labels()`;
`.label()` names the grid.

### Hover cards

```ts
HoverCard(Text(`@${user.handle}`), UserCard(user)).align('start').label(user.name)
```

Bloom's `HoverCard`: the card a resting pointer or focus opens on the web,
and a long press on native. `HoverCard(trigger, content)`: the trigger carries
the view's box, `.label()` names the card. It takes `.presented([open, set])`
(when the screen owns it), `.openDelay(duration)` and `.closeDelay(duration)`
(duration tokens: Bloom's web waits are `'400'` and `fast`), `.side('top' |
'bottom')`, `.align('start' | 'center' | 'end')`, `.padded(false)` (a card
whose content paints its own surface) and `.disabled()`.

### Expandable text

```ts
ExpandableText(review.body).lines(3)
ExpandableText(app.description, [open, setOpen]).style('inline').font('caption-1')
```

Bloom's `ExpandableText`: text clamped to `.lines(n)` (3 by default; 0 never
clamps) with a control to read the rest, drawn only when something is hidden.
`.style('below' | 'inline')` places the control (a "Show more" under it, or a
"More" over the end of its last line that opens it for good), and
`.labels({ more, less })` words it. The binding, when given, holds whether it
is open. Its type and colour are the text environment's (`.font()`,
`.weight()`, `.foreground()`, set on it or above it), as a `Text`'s are: a
control is handed that environment (`ControlDraw.text`).

### Category bars

```ts
CategoryBar('Property type', categories, [type, setType]).trailing(filtersButton).backdrop('surface')
```

Bloom's `CategoryBar`: a scrolling strip of icon-over-label categories.
`CategoryBar(label, items, binding)`: the label names the tablist, each item is
Bloom's `{ key, label, icon }`, the binding holds the selected key (`undefined`
selects none; a press sets it, the selected one's included). It takes
`.trailing(view)` (pinned after the strip, never scrolling), `.spacing()`
(`3xl` by default), `.backdrop(color)` (the surface the web edge fades
blend into) and `.labels({ previous, next })` (the web arrows' names).

### Menubars

```ts
Menubar(
  MenubarMenu('File', Item('New tab').shortcut('Mod+T').tag('newTab'), Divider(), Item('Close').tag('close')),
  MenubarMenu('Edit', Item('Undo').shortcut('Mod+Z').tag('undo')),
).actions(AppActions()).label('Application')
```

Bloom's `Menubar`: its `MenubarMenu`s in a row, one open at a time. A menu's
title is its trigger, its rows are a `Menu`'s, and `.id()` names it (its title
by default); it takes `.disabled()` and `.testID()` (its title's). The bar takes
`.openMenu([id, setId])` (the open menu when the screen owns it, `undefined`
while none is), `.actions()` for its tagged rows, `.label()` and a box. A row's
`.shortcut('Mod+N')` is its key chord: Bloom's menubar draws it beside the
title and runs the row from it, open or closed (the other menus draw no
shortcut). An item's platform action (Undo, Copy…, Settings) and alternates
are not in the vocabulary yet.
### Phone inputs

```ts
PhoneInput([phone, setPhone], 'Phone').country([country, setCountry]).required().description('We text a code to it.')
```

Bloom's `PhoneInput`: a number field with its country-code select. The binding
holds the number as typed, without the dial code; a title is its visible label
and name (`.label()` names it when there is none). It takes
`.country([iso2, set])` (`US` by default), `.countries(list)` (Bloom's
`COUNTRIES` by default), `.placeholder()`, `.description()` (the caption under
it), `.required()`, `.showsInfo()` (the info glyph after the title), `.size()`,
`.invalid()`, `.disabled()`, `.trailingIcon()` and `.labels({ countrySelect })`.

### Composition bars

```ts
CompositionBar(
  [
    { key: 'content', name: 'Content', amount: 320, tint: 'success' },
    { key: 'social', name: 'Social', amount: 180, tint: 'info' },
  ],
  [chosen, setChosen],
  'Tap a segment to see its share',
).readoutFormat((points, percent) => `${points} pts · ${percent}%`)
```

Bloom's `CompositionBar`: what a total is made of, a segment each, its width
its share. `CompositionBar(segments, selection, hint)`: each segment is Bloom's
category with its colour a role (`tint`, a role at an opacity included,
painted from the theme); the binding holds the chosen segment's key (a press
sets it to the pressed one); the hint shows while none is chosen.
`.readoutFormat((amount, percent) => text)` words the readout.

### Input groups

```ts
InputGroup(
  InputGroupAddon('https://'),
  TextField([domain, setDomain], 'Domain').placeholder('oxy'),
  InputGroupAddon(Button(Text('Go')).size('sm').style('subtle')).divider().flush(),
).size('sm')
```

Bloom's `InputGroup` and `InputGroupAddon`: one bordered box around a control
and the things attached to it. A group's children are drawn in order: the
addons before the control lead, the rest trail, and the control (a
`TextField`, a `Search`) draws bare in the group's shell. A group takes
`.size()`, `.invalid()` and `.disabled()`. An addon holds a string (Bloom's
subdued label) or views; it takes `.divider()` (a hairline on its inner edge)
and `.flush()` (no padding of its own, for a control with its own hit area).

### Notifications

```ts
Notification('Deployed').description('Build 412 is live').timestamp('2m ago')
  .status('success').buttons([{ label: 'View', onPress: openBuild }]).onDismiss(clear)
Notification('Ana replied').avatar({ name: 'Ana Ruiz', presence: 'online' }).autoDismiss('4000')
```

Bloom's `Notification`, the one notification card. Its title is a string or a
view; it takes `.description()`, `.timestamp()`,
`.status('neutral' | 'information' | 'success' | 'warning' | 'error')` (its
disc's colour and glyph), `.icon()`, `.avatar()` (Bloom's avatar and its
presence, in place of the disc), `.buttons([...])` (Bloom's actions),
`.dismissible(false)`, `.onDismiss()`, `.autoDismiss(duration)` (with its
countdown bar), `.introDelay(duration)` (its entrance after that wait, `'0'` at
once: a duration token in milliseconds, which the renderer hands Bloom in the
seconds its prop takes), `.liveRole('alert' | 'status')` and
`.labels({ close })`. A self-dismissing notification is compared by Jest only:
in a browser two side by side are caught at different moments of their
countdowns.

### Link previews

```ts
LinkPreview(await oxy.getLinkPreview(url))
LinkPreview({ url, title, image: contentImageSources(cover) }).coverFill().frame({ height: '180' })
```

Bloom's `LinkPreviewCard`: an unfurled link, its cover, site, title,
description and byline. `LinkPreview(preview)` takes the Oxy SDK's link
preview as it comes (its `url`, `title`, `description`, `image` (one URL or
the same image at several widths), `siteName` and `author`). A press opens the
url unless `.onPress()` replaces it; `.coverFill()` lets the cover fill the
height the card is given (a fixed-height row), the text a footer under it.

### Social buttons

```ts
SocialButton('google').style('white').intent('signIn').link(oauthStart('google'))
SocialButton({ icon: <RiKey2Line />, label: 'Acme SSO', color: '#0f766e' }).fullWidth()
```

Bloom's `SocialButton`: a built-in provider's logo, colour and title together,
or a brand of your own (Bloom's brand config: its icon, name and colour).
`SocialButton(brand, title?)`: the title replaces the default ("Continue with
Google", in the app's language). It takes `.size('md' | 'sm')`,
`.style('colorful' | 'black' | 'white')`, `.iconOnly()`, `.fullWidth()`,
`.intent('continue' | 'signIn' | 'signUp')` (the verb of its title),
`.link(href)` (an OAuth start is a navigation), `.onPress()` and
`.disabled()`.

### Data tables

```ts
DataTable(customers, (c) => c.id,
  DataColumn('name', 'Customer', (c) => c.name).share(2),
  DataColumn('price', 'Price', (c) => c.price).align('end').cell(({ row }) => Text(money(row.price))),
)
  .title('Total Results').summary(`${customers.length} customers`)
  .toolbar(DataTableFilter([region, setRegion], 'Region', regions), DataTableSearch([q, setQ], 'Search'))
  .sorting([sort, setSort]).rowSelection([selected, setSelected]).page([page, setPage])
  .sizeToggle([size, setSize]).label('Customers')
```

Bloom's `DataTable`: every row (filtered by the screen), each named by its id
function, in its `DataColumn`s; Bloom sorts, selects and pages them.
`DataColumn(id, header, value?)`: the value is what it sorts by and shows; it
takes `.cell(context => view)`, `.width()`, `.share()`, `.minWidth()`,
`.basis()`, `.align()`, `.sortable(false)`, `.sortsDescendingFirst()` and
`.label()` (its header's name). The table takes `.title()`, `.summary()`,
`.toolbar(...)`, `.sorting([sort, set])`, `.rowSelection([ids, set])`,
`.pageSize()`, `.page([page, set])`, `.size('sm' | 'md')`,
`.sizeToggle([size, set])` (the Normal / Compact control), `.bordered(false)`,
`.emptyState()`, `.minWidth()`, `.style('table' | 'inset')` and `.labels()`.
The toolbar's `DataTableFilter([value, set], label, options)` and
`DataTableSearch([text, set], label)`, and a cell's
`DataTableSelect([value, set], label, options, width)`, are Bloom's own.

### Sortable chip rows

```ts
SortableChipRow(...links.map((l) => SortableChip(l.id, l.title, Text(l.title))))
  .onReorder((ids) => setLinks(ids.map((id) => byId[id])))
  .trailing(Button(Text('Add link')).onPress(addLink)).label('Links')
```

Bloom's `SortableChipRow`: its chips in the screen's order, each a
`SortableChip(key, label, content)` (the key is its identity across moves, the
label what a screen reader says when it is moved). A drag is Gesture Handler 3's
pan, as Bloom's other gestures are; the arrow keys on the web and the screen
reader's actions move a chip by one. Only the order changes: `.onReorder(keys =>
…)` hands the screen the new order to write. It takes `.onDragChange(dragging =>
…)` (to hold a parent's scroll), `.trailing(...)` (after the chips, not part of
the order), `.disabled()` and `.label()`.

### Viewports

```ts
Viewport(Embed(<ReviewsScroll />)).root()          // a dialog's own chain
const Histogram = view(() => {
  const { inView, targetProps } = useInView({ threshold: 1, once: true });
  return Embed((render) => <View {...targetProps}>{render(RatingBar('Five stars', 75).reveal({ visible: inView }))}</View>);
});
```

Bloom's `viewport` is a provider and two hooks, and the language takes them as
they are. `Viewport(...)` is Bloom's `ViewportProvider`, a view around what it
holds with no box of its own; `.root()` starts a chain of its own (a portaled
dialog's). Its hooks (`useViewportBinding` on the scroller, `useInView` on the
target) are used as they are inside a `view()`: they hand the props a native
host takes (its ref and its layout and scroll callbacks), so the host they go on
is an `Embed`.

## Product components

The product families apps share (agents, chat, dashboards, media), and those
no app imports yet (commerce, the dashboard cards and
a few more), are Bloom's own components drawn as nodes. A node takes its content first and its data as
an options object, as the Bloom component takes them: a slot is a language
child, a handler a function, and the data is Bloom's own type, so Bloom's
constants and an app's existing data fit as they are (a node inside data, a
suggestion's `leading`, is a React element: `toElement(view)`). Its look is modifiers, checked by the spec: a
size, a style or a tone is a token, a colour a role (painted where it is
drawn), a length a space token, a duration (a reveal's pace, a count-up, a
cross-fade) a duration token, and each value it edits beside its main one a
binding modifier (`.expanded([open, setOpen])`, see [Inputs](#inputs)), never
a value and its `on…Change` in the options. The universal modifiers place, box and name
it, and `.onPress()` puts a press on a component that takes none of its own.

### Colour lists

```ts
ComposerLoader(Composer(), { phase }).gradient(['primary', 'info/half', brand.color.sky, 'success'])
ChatBackground(Thread()).style('gradient').gradient(['primary', 'secondary'])
StoryRing(Avatar(friend.avatar), { size: '56' }).gradient(['accent'])
```

A component that takes a list of colours (a composer loader's four, a chat
background's gradient stops, a story ring's colours) takes it as
`.gradient([...])`: each colour a role, a role at an opacity, or an app's own
colour (`defineTokens({ color })`), never a raw value. The compiler checks
each one written out. Bloom blends a list (a loader's midpoint, a gradient's
stops), so its roles are painted from the theme's RGB colours, as an app hands
them over from `useTheme().colors`, in the leaf that draws the component.

### Agent avatar

```ts
AgentAvatar({ preset: 'blue_beret' }).size('xl').mood('sad').label('Ana')
AgentAvatar(agent.character, { workingKey, entranceKey }).interactive()
```

`AgentAvatar(character, options?)` is Bloom's `AgentAvatar`: the vector
character, drawn by Bloom's renderer on every platform (the native one
included, under the same props). The options are its triggers and reports:
`reactionKey`, `idleKey`, `entranceKey`, `workingKey` and `workingCycles`
play a motion when they change; `onReady`, `onLoadingChange`, `catalog` and
`locale`.

- `.size()` is an avatar rung or an app token; with none, Bloom's 64.
- `.mood('neutral' | 'sad')`, `.paused(true)`, `.interactive()` (its gaze
  follows the pointer and touches), `.portrait()` (a cached still, for a
  picker's thumbnails).
- `AgentAvatarProvider` stays the app's: it is where the vector catalog comes from.

### Chat composer

```ts
ChatComposer([text, setText], { placeholder: 'Message', onSend: send, onAttachPress: attach,
  banner: replyTo ? ComposerBanner('reply', replyTo.author, { preview: replyTo.text, onClose: clear }) : undefined })
CompactComposer([text, setText], { maxLength: 280, onSubmit: post, avatar: Avatar(me.avatar) })
ComposerIconButton(MicIcon, 'Record').tint('accent').size('4xl').onPress(record)
MessageContextMenu(Bubble({ message }), items, { onSelect, reactions: ['❤️', '👍'] })
```

Bloom's `chat-composer` family. A composer writes the text its binding holds
(`ChatComposer`'s `value`/`onValueChange`, `CompactComposer`'s
`value`/`onChangeText`); its slots (`notice`, `banner`, `leading`,
`emojiSlot`, `recorder`; `avatar`, `attachments`) are language children.
`.disabled()` disables either.

- `ComposerBanner(kind, title, options?)`: `reply`, `edit`, `forward` or
  `note`; `.tint(role)` its accent.
- `ComposerIconButton(icon, label, options?)`: `.tint('plain' | 'accent')`,
  `.size()` a space token (36 by default), `.iconSize()` an icon rung,
  `.disabled()`; `.onPress()` and `.onLongPress()` are its own.
- `SuggestionList(kind, suggestions, options?)`: `mention`, `command` or
  `emoji`; `.size()` a control size, `.loading()`; its `maxHeight` a space token.
- `VoiceRecorder(state, seconds, options?)`: `recording`, `locked` or `preview`.
- `AttachmentMenu(trigger, options?)`: the trigger is a child (a `Button`);
  `.style('grid' | 'list')`, `.columns(n)`.
- `EmojiPicker(groups, options?)`: `.size()` a control size; its `height` a
  space token.
- `ReactionPicker(emojis?, options?)`: `.size('sm' | 'md')`, `.surface(false)`.
- `MessageContextMenu(message, items, options?)`: the message is the child it
  opens on.

### Chart cards

```ts
LineChartCard({ title: 'Revenue', data, ranges, onRangeChange }).shape('sharp').tint('primary')
BarListCard({ title: 'Sources', items, stats }).tint('success')
ChartCardSurface([ChartHeadline('Revenue', { value, delta }).id('h'), Sparkline(points).tint('positive').id('s')]).spacing('sm')
```

Bloom's `chart-cards` family. A card takes its data as options, as Bloom's
does (its title, points, series, ranges, stats and their handlers); data a
card cannot be drawn without comes first (`SleepScoreCard(metrics)`,
`StepsCard(points)`, `TokensChartCard(points)`, `Sparkline(values)`,
`ChartHeadline(label)`, `ChartStatTiles(tiles)`). `ChartStatTiles` takes
`.activeIndex([index, setIndex])`, the hovered tile (`null` none) as a
binding: Bloom's `activeIndex`, and its `onActiveChange` writing it.

- `AreaChartCard`: `.style('stacked' | 'overlap' | 'percent')`,
  `.shape('curved' | 'sharp')`. `LineChartCard`: `.shape()`.
  `RadarChartCard`: `.style('filled' | 'dots' | 'lines' | 'score')`,
  `.legend('bottom' | 'top' | 'overlay')`. `BarListCard` and `StageBarsCard`:
  `.mono()`, one ink for every bar.
- A card's own colour is `.tint(role)`, its active point's `.activeTint(role)`
  (`BarListCard` and `ContributionsCard` take a tint; `HeatmapChartCard`,
  `LineChartCard`, `StepsCard` and `TokensChartCard` both). A series' or a
  stage's colour is its data's, as Bloom takes it.
- `Sparkline(values, options?)`: `.tint('accent' | 'positive' | 'negative' |
  'neutral')`, `.shape('curved' | 'sharp')`, `.filled()`; its `width` and
  `height` are space tokens, as are a sleep
  card's `height` and a tokens card's `plotHeight`.
- `ChartCardSurface(content, options?)`: the card the others are drawn on (a
  list of views is its children, keyed by `.id()`); `.spacing()` its gap, its
  `height` a space token or `auto`, its `radius` a card's corner token (as
  `Card(...).radius()`).

### Composer panel

```ts
ComposerPanel([prompt, setPrompt], { onSubmit: send, providers, permissions, addMenu }).model([model, setModel])
ComposerPill([prompt, setPrompt], { onSubmit: send, models: ['Fast', 'Deep'] }).surface(false)
ComposerPanelStatusTab({ branch: 'main', project: 'bloom', context: 42 })
```

Bloom's `composer-panel` family: the agent composer writes the prompt its
binding holds (`value`/`onValueChange`). The options are its data as Bloom
takes them (Bloom's `COMPOSER_PANEL_ADD_MENU` and `COMPOSER_PANEL_PERMISSIONS`
fit as they are); `question`, `status` and `emptyAction` are language
children. `.disabled()` disables either composer; `.surface(false)` draws the
pill on a surface of yours, `.frosted()` frosts it.

### AI chat

```ts
AiChatShell(
  AiChatContainer(
    AiChatThread(turns.map((turn) =>
      turn.mine
        ? AiChatUserMessage(AiChatMessageLine(turn.text)).id(turn.id)
        : AiChatAssistantMessage(turn.lines.map((line, i) => AiChatMessageLine(line).id(i)), { feedback: true }).id(turn.id),
    )),
    { title: chat.title, composer: ComposerPill([prompt, setPrompt], { onSubmit: send }) },
  ),
  { sidebar: Conversations(), panel: (width) => AiChatCodePanel(code, { changedFiles }) },
)
```

Bloom's `ai-chat` family. A container takes its content first and its data as
options: `AiChatShell` (its `sidebar`, `mobileSidebar`, `background` children,
its `panel` a function of the panel's width returning one, its widths space
tokens), `AiChatContainer` (`actions`, `header`, `composer`, `background`),
`AiChatThread` (its turns), `AiChatUserMessage` and `AiChatAssistantMessage`
(their lines; `actions`, `feedback`, `feedbackProps`, `animate`). A list of
content is the component's children as Bloom takes them, one each, keyed by
`.id()`.

- Their looks are modifiers: a shell's `.sidebarSurface('card' | 'plain' |
  'docked')`, `.sidebarScrollbarSide('end' | 'start')` and `.surface(false)`;
  a container's `.surface(false)` and `.floatingChrome()`.

- `AiChatMessageLine(content)`: a string is the line's text; a view is a
  block of the reply (Bloom's `block`). `.tint('primary' | 'secondary')`,
  `.selectable(false)`.
- `AiChatHeader(content)`, `AiChatMobileHeader(title)`.
- `AiChatCodePanel(code, options?)` (its `browser` a child) and
  `AiChatGalleryPanel(generations, options?)` (its `stylePresets` a child);
  their `width` is a length (a space token or a fraction of the parent).

### Stat cards

```ts
StatCards(kpis).style('footer').columns(3)
StatCard({ icon: UsersIcon, label: 'Users', value: '14,592', delta: '+5.3%', deltaColor: 'lime' })
```

Bloom's `stat-cards`: `StatCard(stat)` and `StatCards(stats)` take Bloom's
`StatCardsItem`s. `.style('plain' | 'footer')`; `.columns(1 | 2 | 3 | 4)`, the
grid's columns at its widest. Bloom's `count` is `stats.slice(0, n)`.

### Media controls

```ts
PlayButton(playing, { subject: track.title }).size('lg').onPress(toggle)
PlaybackProgress(position, duration, { onSeek: seek, showTimes: true }).style('below')
```

Bloom's `media-controls`: `PlayButton(playing, options?)` (`subject`,
`playLabel`, `pauseLabel`, `interactive`): `.size('sm' | 'md' | 'lg')`,
`.style('accent' | 'inverse' | 'plain')`, `.loading()`, `.disabled()`;
`.onPress()` and `.onLongPress()` are its own. `PlaybackProgress(value,
duration, options?)` (`buffered`, `onSeek`, `onSeekPreview`, `showTimes`,
`showRemaining`, `keyboardStep`, `formatValueText`): `.style('inline' |
'below')` places its times, `.disabled()`.

### Agents

```ts
AgentThinking(Text(reasoning), { thinking, durationMs }).style('stars').tint('accent')
AgentLogToolCalls(calls.map((call) => AgentLogToolCall(call.name, call.status, { detail: call.detail }).id(call.id)))
ComposerLoader(ComposerPill([prompt, setPrompt]), { phase })
UsageCard({ usage, plan, limits, footer: Button(Text('Upgrade')) })
```

The agent families two or more apps share, each Bloom's own:
`AgentLimitsCard(options)`, `UsageCard(options)` (its `fallback` and
`footer` children, its `radius` a card's corner token), `AgentThinking(reasoning?, options?)` (`.style('wave' |
'spin' | 'stars' | 'infinity')`, `.tint('subtle' | 'default' | 'primary' |
'accent')`), `AgentProgress(options)`, `AgentProgressLoadingText(text)`
(`.lines(n)`, `.textVariant(step)` a step of Bloom's ramp, `body-regular` by
default),
`AgentLogRow(content, { first, last })`, `AgentLogShimmerText(text)`,
`AgentLogToolCall(name, status, options?)` (`running`, `done` or `error`;
its `content` what it shows expanded), `AgentLogToolCalls(calls)`,
`AgentLogWorkingRow(label)`, `ComposerLoader(content, options?)` (round the
composer; its `radius` a corner token, its `line` and `offset` space tokens),
`ConversationSidebar(conversations, active, options?)` (`active` the open
conversation's id, a binding: Bloom's `activeId` and its `onSelect`; its
`after` a child; `.destination([id, setId])` the selected destination or tree
row of the sidebar's own rows, a binding drawn as a `Sidebar`'s
`.selection()`) and
`Questionnaire(questions, options?)`.

- `ComposerLoader(...).gradient([c0, c1, c2, c3])` is the loader's four
  colours, left to right (see [Colour lists](#colour-lists)); with none, Bloom's
  own iridescent set.
- A component's secondary looks are modifiers: a composer loader's
  `.blend()` (CSS's blend modes), `.bloomOnly()` and `.surface(false)`; a
  conversation sidebar's `.style('panel' | 'rail')`, `.size('sm' | 'md' |
  'lg')`, `.sidebarSurface('card' | 'plain' | 'docked')`,
  `.contentAlignment('start' | 'center')`, `.primaryActionPlacement('bottom'
  | 'top')`, `.railLabels('visible' | 'hidden')`, `.railSelection('pill' |
  'icon')`, `.modesPresentation('segmented' | 'toggle')`,
  `.scrollbarSide('end' | 'start')` and `.teamPlacement('footer' | 'header')`.
- The thinking marks and the working row move on the JS thread while the
  agent works; their cases are compared by Jest as they mount, not in Chrome,
  where two side by side are read at different frames.

### Chat

```ts
ChatHeader(chat.name, { status, avatarSource: chat.avatar, onPressBack: back }).tint('accent')
ChatHeader(chat.name, { status }).presentation('bar').titleLayout('stacked').titleAlign('center').dividers(false)
ChatBackground(Thread(), { source: wallpaper }).style('image')
ImageMessage(photo.url, { aspectRatio: photo.ratio, caption: Text(photo.caption) }).tint('outgoing')
StoryRing(Avatar(friend.avatar), { size: '56', state: 'unseen' }).onPress(openStory)
```
