The person-to-person chat families two or more apps share: the screen's
parts (`ChatBackground`, `ChatDateHeader`, `ChatEmptyState`, `ChatHeader`,
`ChatInfoPanel`, `PinnedMessageBar`, `ScrollToBottomButton`), the list's
(`ChatFolderTabs`, `ChatSearchField`, `ChatSearchResults`, `StoriesRow`), the
indicators (`StoryRing`, `TypingDots`) and the media in a bubble
(`ContactMessage`, `DocumentGrid`, `FileMessage`, `GifMessage`,
`ImageMessage`, `LocationMessage`, `PollMessage`, `SharedMediaGrid`,
`StickerMessage`, `VideoMessage`, `VoiceMessage`).

- A component's main variant is `.style()` (a background's, a date header's
  placement, an info panel's, a file's, a video's); its tone `.tint()` (a
  header's status, and a bubble's media `incoming` or `outgoing`); a bubble's
  own colours `.bubbleTint()` and `.contentTint()`, and media that loads
  `.placeholderTint()`, all roles. A row's gap is `.spacing()`.
- Lengths in the options (widths, sizes, offsets, paddings) are space tokens,
  a radius a corner rung, a background's opacities opacity tokens. Its slots
  (an empty state's action, a header's avatar and accessory, an image's
  caption, a ring's badge, a map from `renderMap`) are language children.
- A header's secondary looks are modifiers too: `.presentation()` (Bloom's
  `floating`, `bar` or `navigation`), `.titleAlign('start' | 'center')`,
  `.titleLayout('inline' | 'stacked')`, `.titleAppearance('plain' |
  'capsule')`, `.density('compact' | 'default')` (with neither, the window's
  width decides) and `.dividers(false)`. A pinned bar's dismiss glyph is
  `.dismissIcon('close' | 'unpin')`; folder tabs and a document grid draw their
  hairlines with `.dividers()`.
- `ChatSearchField(query)` writes its binding; `ChatFolderTabs` is named with
  `.label()`; `ScrollToBottomButton` and `StoryRing` take `.onPress()`
  themselves. `PollMessage(question, choices, options?)`: the choices are
  Bloom's `options`.
- `.gradient([...])` is a background's gradient stops (two or more, for its
  `gradient` style) and a story ring's colours (one for a solid ring, two or
  more for a gradient); see [Colour lists](#colour-lists). The typing dots
  pulse on the JS thread: compared by Jest, not in Chrome.

### Listings, maps and media

```ts
ListingHeader(home.title, { rating, location, actions: ListingHeaderAction('Save', { icon: HeartIcon }).onPress(save) }).size('medium')
MapPriceMarker(home.price).style(selected ? 'active' : 'default').onPress(select)
Shelf('New releases', releases.map((r) => Release(r).id(r.id)), { onShowAll }).style('grid').spacing('md')
FollowButton([following, setFollowing]).size('sm')
SubscriptionPlans([plan, setPlan], { productName: 'Oxy', plans, selectorLabel: 'Plan' })
```

The listing details (`AmenityList`, `HostCard`, `ListingHeader`
`.size('large' | 'medium')`, `ListingHeaderAction`, `ListingPhotoGrid`
`.style('auto' | 'grid' | 'carousel')`, `ReviewCard` `.lines(n)`), the map
markers (`MapClusterMarker` and `MapPriceMarker` `.style('default' | 'active' |
'visited')`, a price marker `.size('default' | 'compact')`,
`MapSearchAreaButton` a button or a toggle, `.disabled()`), `FollowButton`
(its binding whether one follows; `.style()` and `.tint()` a button's,
`.size('sm' | 'md' | 'lg')`, `.loading()`), `Shelf(title, items, options?)`
and `ShelfSkeleton` (`.style('row' | 'grid' | 'paged')`, `.spacing()`; a
skeleton's `.round()` covers and `.eyebrow()`),
`SubscriptionPlans(plan, options)` (its binding the chosen plan; its hero,
controls, terms, footer and fallback children), `SubscriptionPrice(price)`
and `UserHoverCard(displayName, options?)` (its action, badge and footer
children). The markers, the header action and the follow button take
`.onPress()` themselves. A follow button's `.material('surface' | 'flat' |
'shared' | 'lens')` is the shared Button's material; it and the header
action take `.iconOnly()`.

### Card form

```ts
CardForm([card, setCard], { countries, errors }).disabled(paying)
CardFormHostedField('Card number', focused, { content: (control) => Embed(<ProviderField {...control} />) })
```

Bloom's `card-form`: `CardForm(card, options?)` edits the card its binding
holds (a payment provider's `hostedFields` are language children);
`CardFormCountry(country, countries, options?)` and
`CardFormPostcode(postcode, options?)` are parts an app lays out itself;
`CardFormHostedField(label, focused, options)` frames a provider's field,
its `content` a function of the field's control. Each takes `.disabled()`.

### Checkout sheet

```ts
CheckoutSheet({ open, step, direction, request, draft, onDraft, onStep, onBack, onClose, onAuthorize,
  savedCards, onCard, label: t, formatAmount, cardEntry: ProviderCardFields() })
```

Bloom's `checkout-sheet`: `CheckoutSheet(options)` is controlled by the app
as Bloom's is (its request, draft, step and their handlers, its words and
amounts); `methodContent`, `footer`, `resultIllustration`, `cardEntry` and
`notice` are language children. The method step's title is
`.methodTitleAlign('left' | 'center')` and `.methodTitleVariant(step)`, a step
of Bloom's type ramp. Its open sheet is compared by Jest; in Chrome it is
presented outside the parity table's cells.

### Stay filters

```ts
FilterSection('Price range', PriceRangeFilter([range, setRange], { min: 20, max: 1000, buckets }).style('log'))
CountFilter('Bedrooms', [bedrooms, setBedrooms], { max: 5 })
AmenityFilter(amenities, [chosen, setChosen], { collapsedCount: 6 }).label('Amenities')
FeatureFilter([features, setFeatures]).style('checkboxes')
FilterFooter(t('Show {n} places', { n }), { onApply: apply, onClear: clear }).loading(counting)
FilterTriggerButton(applied).size('sm').onPress(openFilters)
```

Bloom's `stay-filters`. Each filter edits the value its binding holds (a
range, a count, a selection, a switch); `AvailabilityFilter(availableNow)`
takes its day as `.date([day, setDay])`, which Bloom requires. `FilterSection(title,
content, options?)` takes its controls as its content and `.dividers(false)`
on the last one. A chip filter's chips are Bloom's `ToggleChipOption`s: the
first argument of `ToggleChipGroup` and `AmenityFilter`, the `choices` option
of `FeatureFilter` and `FloorFilter` (Bloom's `options`); name a group with
`.label()`.

- `PriceRangeFilter(range, options)`: `.style('linear' | 'log')` is how the
  slider spreads its prices; its `histogramHeight` a space token.
- `FeatureFilter`: `.style('chips' | 'checkboxes')`.
- `FilterTriggerButton(count?, options?)`: `.size('sm' | 'md' | 'lg')`;
  `.onPress()` is its own.
- `FilterFooter(resultsLabel, options)`: `.loading()` while the count is
  fetched.
- Every filter takes `.disabled()`.

### Stay search

```ts
StaySearchCompact({ summary: 'Anywhere · Any week', onFilterPress: openFilters }).onPress(openSearch)
StaySearchStep('Where', DestinationSuggestions(places, { onSelect: choose }), { expanded: step === 'where', summary }).onPress(() => setStep('where'))
StaySearchPanel(GuestPicker([guests, setGuests], { note: Text(t('At most 4 guests')) }).size('sm'), { width: '320' }).label('Who')
```

Bloom's `stay-search`, as a narrow screen draws it. `StaySearchCompact(options?)`
and `StaySearchStep(label, content, options)` take `.onPress()` themselves (the
step's `expanded` is the app's); `StaySearchPanel(content, options?)` is the
card a step's content drops into, its `width` and `padding` space tokens and
`.label()` naming it a dialog. `DestinationSuggestions(items, options)` takes
Bloom's suggestions and their `onSelect`. `GuestPicker(guests, options?)` edits
the counts its binding holds: `.size('sm' | 'md')` its steppers, its `note` a
language child.

### Tenancy

```ts
LeaseSummaryCard({ ...lease, actions: Button(Text('Pay rent')).onPress(pay) }).style('wide')
RentPaymentList(payments, { outstanding: '€1,150' }).tint(late ? 'error' : 'default')
TenancyTimeline(events).density('compact').label(t('Tenancy history'))
```

Bloom's `tenancy`: `TenancyTimeline(events, options?)`, `LeaseSummaryCard(options)`,
`RentPaymentList(payments, options?)`, `MaintenanceRequestCard(options)` and
`DocumentList(documents, options?)`, each taking Bloom's own data. A card's,
list's or document list's layout is `.style('auto' | 'wide' | 'narrow')`; the
outstanding amount's tone `.tint('default' | 'error')`; the timeline's rows
`.density('comfortable' | 'compact')`. The
lease's and the request's `actions` are language children.

### Evictions and place reviews

```ts
EvictionReportCard({ ...report, attending: [attending, setAttending], onShare: share }).lines(3)
EvictionTimeline(events).label(t('What happened'))
WriteReviewPrompt(building.title, { onStart: review, media: Image(building.photo) })
```

Bloom's `eviction`: `EvictionReportCard(options)` (whether one attends is its
`attending` binding; `.lines(n)` cuts the description) and
`EvictionTimeline(events, options?)`. Bloom's `place-reviews`:
`PlaceReviewSummary(rating, options?)` and `WriteReviewPrompt(buildingTitle,
options)` (its `media` a language child). `PlaceReviewCard`, which no app
imports, is not drawn.

### Project board

```ts
ProjectBoard(columns, { getItemId: (row) => row.id, dragMode: 'handle', onMove: move,
  renderCard: (row) => ProjectBoardCardContent(undefined, {
    header: BoardDragHandle(Text(row.title)).label(`Move ${row.title}`).onPress(() => open(row)).grow(),
    description: row.summary,
  }),
  columnWidth: '256' }).label(view.name)
GroupedProjectBoard(lanes, { getItemId, onLaneCollapsedChange, renderLaneHeader: (lane) => Text(lane.title) })
ProjectBoardTemplate({ title: 'Launch', initialColumns, members, headerActions: Inbox() })
```

Bloom's `project-board`: one board, three ways of holding it.
`ProjectBoard(columns, options)` is Bloom's embedded board (the app owns its
items and their moves), `GroupedProjectBoard(lanes, options)` stacks boards in
collapsible lanes, and `ProjectBoardTemplate(options)` is the ticket board with
its own header, tickets and dialogs. The options are Bloom's data as it takes
it (how an item is read, its moves, `readOnly`, `dragMode`); a render prop
(`renderCard`, `renderColumnHeader`…, `renderLaneHeader`) returns a view, and
`columnWidth` is a space token.

- `ProjectBoardCardContent(content, options?)`: a card's body in the board's
  card geometry; `preview`, `actions`, `badges` and `footer` are views, and
  `header`, `title` and `description` a string (drawn in the card's own text
  style) or a view.
- `BoardDragHandle(content)`: what a card is dragged by in `dragMode:
  'handle'`; `.label()` names it, `.onPress()` opens the card.

### Activity feed

```ts
ActivityFeed(entries, { emptyLabel: t('empty') }).style('none').density('compact').label('Updates')
```

Bloom's `activity-feed`: `ActivityFeed(entries, options?)` takes Bloom's
`ActivityFeedEntry`s (a title, context, content or action inside one is a
React element: `toElement(view)`). `.style('day' | 'none')` groups the entries
under day headings or none, `.density('comfortable' | 'compact')` is its
rhythm, `.lines(n)` the lines a body clamps to.

### Call screens

```ts
CallScreen(peer.name, call.status, { duration, avatar: peer.avatar, controls: { onEndCall: end } }).muted([muted, setMuted]).style('video').tint('primary')
CallMinimisedPill(peer.name, { duration, onExpand: expand, onEndCall: end }).style(call.mode)
GroupCallGrid(participants, { formatMuted }).style('spotlight').columns(2).spacing('xs').aspectRatio(16 / 9)
IncomingCallScreen(peer.name, { onAccept: answer, onDecline: decline }).answerStyle('slide')
IncomingCallBanner(peer.name, { onAccept: answer, onDecline: decline }).onPress(open)
```

Bloom's `call-ui`: a call's medium is `.style('voice' | 'video')` and the hue
its stage is washed with `.tint(role)` (the theme's accent by default), on
`CallScreen(name, status, options?)`, `CallMinimisedPill(name?, options?)`,
`IncomingCallScreen(name, options?)` and `IncomingCallBanner(name, options?)`.
The options are the call's data as Bloom takes it: its pre-formatted time,
words, handlers and a minimised screen; what the screen edits is a binding
(the toggles, the picture-in-picture's corner).

- `CallScreen`: its video frames (`remoteVideo`, `localVideo`), a control bar
  of the app's own (`controlsSlot`), the top bar's extra chrome (`topBarSlot`)
  and `content` (drawn over the stage) are language children; its end-to-end
  line is a string or a view; the picture-in-picture's width is a space token.
  `controls` is Bloom's control bar's actions, order and words, its buttons
  sized by the screen's `.size('sm' | 'md' | 'lg')`, its extra buttons
  `extra` (children). Its toggles are the screen's bindings, `.muted()`,
  `.speaker()`, `.videoOn()` and `.screenSharing()`, drawn into the bar as
  Bloom's value and handler (a bound toggle draws its button, as a handler
  does in Bloom); with neither `controls` nor a toggle there is no bar.
  `.localVideoCorner([corner, setCorner])` is the picture-in-picture's corner
  (Bloom's `localVideoCorner`, and its `onMoveLocal` writing the next).
- `GroupCallGrid(participants, options?)`: `.style('grid' | 'spotlight')`,
  `.columns(n)`, `.spacing()` its gutter, `.aspectRatio()` a tile's; a fixed
  `width` is a space token. A participant's colour and video are its data's.
- `IncomingCallScreen`: `.answerStyle('buttons' | 'slide')`; `content` is
  what it draws under its buttons. `IncomingCallBanner` takes `.onPress()`
  itself (its body opens the call).
- A speaking ring and the slide track's chevrons breathe on a loop: those
  cases are compared by Jest as they mount, not in Chrome.

### People beside a chat

```ts
StoryViewer(friend.stories, index, { name: friend.name, avatar: friend.avatar, time, onNext, onPrevious, onClose })
SelectedChipsRow(picked, { onRemove: unpick, emptyState: Text('Nobody yet') }).style('wrap')
NewGroupForm([groupName, setGroupName], { members: picked, onAddMembers: pick })
```

Bloom's `chat-people`: `StoryViewer(stories, index, options)` takes the
author, time, playback state and handlers as Bloom does (a story's `media` is
a React element: `toElement(view)`); a composer of the app's own is a
language child, its `height` a space token. `SelectedChipsRow(people,
options?)` lays its chips out with `.style('scroll' | 'wrap')`; what it shows
with nobody picked is a child. `NewGroupForm(name, options?)` writes the
group's name its binding holds; `content` is the rows it draws between the
fields and the members (Bloom's children). The story's progress bars run on
a timer unless `paused`, so its cases hold them still.

### Message bubbles

```ts
MessageBubble('outgoing', message.text, { time, status, reactions, onToggleReaction: react }).groupPosition('last').tail()
MessageBubble('incoming', undefined, { media: Image(photo.url), time }).mediaFit('inset').metaOverlay()
MessageList(items, { showAvatars: group, showSenderNames: group, unreadLabel: t('unread') })
TypingBubble({ senderName: 'Ana' }).label('Ana is typing')
```

Bloom's `message-bubble`. `MessageBubble(direction, content?, options?)`:
`incoming` or `outgoing` first, then its text (a string, which Bloom links)
or a view of rich content; the options are its sender, reply, reactions,
time, state, handlers and words as Bloom takes them, its `media` a language
child, its `maxWidth` a space token. Its look is modifiers:
`.groupPosition('single' | 'first' | 'middle' | 'last')` (where it sits in
its sender's run), `.mediaFit('bleed' | 'inset' | 'bare')`, `.tail()`,
`.metaOverlay()` (its time over its media) and `.senderTint(role)` (the
sender's name, painted from a role); `.onPress()` and `.onLongPress()` are
its own. `MessageList(items, options?)` takes Bloom's `MessageListItem`s
(a media is a React element: `toElement(view)`), its avatars' size a space
token. `TypingBubble(options?)` is named with `.label()`.

- The typing dots pulse and a highlighted bubble flashes on the JS thread:
  those cases are compared by Jest as they mount, not in Chrome.
### Task list, web search and voice

```ts
TaskList(tasks, { revealed: events.length, collapseOnComplete: true, onComplete: done })
WebSearch(steps, { revealed, labels: { sources: 'Sources' } })
VoiceControls(session.state, { onEnd: hangUp, trailing: Text(elapsed) }).muted([muted, setMuted]).size('md')
```

Bloom's `task-list`, `web-search` and `voice`, as Gemies draws them.
`TaskList(tasks, options?)` and `WebSearch(steps, options?)` take Bloom's
`TaskListTask`s and `WebSearchStep`s (a chip's or a source's `icon` is a React
element: `toElement(view)`), and their reveal as Bloom takes it: its pace, or
`revealed` counted from real events, `working`, `onComplete`, `reduce`.
`VoiceControls(state?, options?)` takes the session's state (`listening` by
default) and its handlers, each showing its control; `leading` and `trailing`
are language children. `.size('sm' | 'md' | 'lg')`, `.disabled()`.

- The working row that trails a running log moves on the JS thread: compared
  by Jest as it mounts, not in Chrome.

### Map chrome

```ts
MapScaleBar([{ width: 72, label: '500 m' }]).style('inline')
MapControls({ onLocate: locate, following, heading, onResetNorth: north, onZoomIn, onZoomOut, islands: Layers() }).align('start')
LocationPuck({ state: 'located', heading, accuracyRadius }).style('compass')
NavigationBanner('right', 'Carrer del Roure', { distance: '400 m', extras: Lanes() })
```

What a map draws over its tiles, each Bloom's own: `MapScaleBar(scales,
options?)` (`.style('island' | 'inline')`), `MapControls(options?)` (each
control drawn when its handler is given; `.tilted([tilted, setTilted])` the
tilt, a binding that draws the tilt button; `.axis()` lays the groups in a row or
a column, `.align('start' | 'end')` picks the edge; its `islands`, a layer
picker or one of the app's, are language children), `LocationPuck(options?)`
(its state and heading are data; `.style('following' | 'compass' |
'navigating')` is what the map does; its `coneLength` a space token, its
`accuracyRadius` the app's projection, in pixels) and
`NavigationBanner(maneuver, instruction, options?)` (its `extras`, lanes or a
speed limit, are language children).

- A locating puck pulses on the UI thread: its cases are compared by Jest as
  it mounts, not in Chrome.

### Places

```ts
PlaceCard(place.name, { category, rating, openState, actions, badge: 'New' }).style('detail').onPress(open)
PlaceHours(days, { state: 'open', summary: 'Open until 20:00' })
PlaceListCard(list.name, { count, visibility: 'shared', empty: Text('No places yet') }).tint('primary').onPress(openList)
DirectionsSteps(route.legs, { currentStepId, onPressStep: pan }).size('compact')
PlacePicker('Address', [place, setPlace], { results, status }).query([query, setQuery]).size('sm')
```

A place and what a map app shows of it, each Bloom's own: `PlaceCard(name,
options?)` (`.style('row' | 'detail')`, `.loading()` its skeleton; its `badge`
a word Bloom draws or a language child), the details (`PlaceHours(days,
options?)`, `PlaceInfoList(items, options?)`, `PlaceTransit(stops, options?)`,
its `footer` a child), a saved list (`PlaceListCard(name, options?)`,
`.tint(role)` the list's own colour; `PlaceList(places, options?)`,
`.disabled()`; their `empty` a child), `DirectionsSteps(legs, options?)`
(`.size('comfortable' | 'compact')`) and `PlacePicker(label, place,
options)`, which holds the chosen place in its binding and the text it
searches in `.query(binding)`, which Bloom requires (`.size()` a control
size, `.disabled()`). The card and the list card
take `.onPress()` themselves. A transit line's colour is the operator's, so it
stays the line's data.

### Mail and notes

```ts
MailComposeSurface(Editor(), { title: 'New message', onSend: send, header: Recipients() }).style('sheet').loading(sending)
MailRecipientField([to, setTo], { label: 'To', suggestions }).query([query, setQuery])
MailThread(messages, { subject, labels, collapseAfter: 3, messageGap: 'md', quickReply: Reply() })
MailMessage(message.sender, Body(), { date, preview, trimmed: Quote() })
NoteEditorToolbar('Formatting', actions).size('sm')
```

Bloom's mail and note pieces: `MailComposeSurface(body?, options?)` round the
message's editor (`.style('docked' | 'sheet')`, `.loading()` while it sends;
its `header`, `toolbar` and `footer` language children),
`MailRecipientField(recipients, options?)`, which edits the recipients its
binding holds (`.disabled()`; its `trailing` a child), `MailThread(messages,
options?)` (its `quickReply` and `header` children, its `messageGap` a space
token; a message's own body inside the data is a React element, as Bloom takes
it), `MailMessage(sender, body?, options?)` (its `trimmed` quote, which it folds
behind its own toggle, and its `menu` are children), `MailQuoteToggle(quote?,
options?)` (a quote folded anywhere else) and `NoteEditorToolbar(label,
actions, options?)`, named as Bloom requires (`.size('md' | 'sm')`,
`.disabled()`).

### Booking

```ts
BookingCard(stay.price, guests, { priceUnit: 'night', checkIn, checkOut, onPressDates, onReserve,
  breakdown: { rows, total } }).loading(reserving)
BookingBar(stay.price, { dates, onPressDates, onReserve, bottomInset: 'lg' }).disabled(!stay.range)
TripCard(trip.title, { image, dates, status: trip.status, actions: Button(Text('Message host')) }).style('auto').onPress(open)
PriceBreakdown(rows, { total: '$915' })
```

Bloom's `booking`: `BookingCard(price, guests, options?)` and `BookingBar(price,
options?)` take the stay's words and handlers as Bloom does; `.loading()` spins
the reserve button and `.disabled()` disables it. A card's `guestPicker`,
`note` (`null` hides it) and `footer` are language children; a bar's
`bottomInset` is a space token. `TripCard(title, options?)`: `.style('auto' |
'horizontal' | 'vertical')` is its orientation, `.onPress()` makes the whole
card its button, and its `actions` are a child. `PriceBreakdown(rows,
options?)` takes Bloom's rows.

### Home search

```ts
HomeSearchBar(segments, [open, setOpen], { onSearch: search, panel: open === 'budget' ? BudgetPanel() : undefined })
SearchModeTabs([mode, setMode]).style('segmented')
PropertyTypePicker([types, setTypes], { labels }).columns(2)
SavedSearchCard(search.title, { criteria, newCount, onEdit, onDelete }).onPress(openSearch)
```

Bloom's `home-search`: `HomeSearchBar(segments, active, options?)` writes the
open segment its binding holds (`null` at rest), its `panel` a language child;
`SearchModeTabs(mode, options?)` writes the mode, `.style('tabs' |
'segmented')` its look; `PropertyTypePicker(types, options?)` writes the
selected types, `.columns(n)` its tiles per row and `.disabled()`;
`SavedSearchCard(title, options?)` takes `.onPress()` itself.

### Listing actions

```ts
ListingActionBar(home.price, 'Apply', { priceUnit: 'month', onPrimary: apply, bottomInset: 'lg' }).loading(applying)
RentalActionCard(home.price, { priceUnit: 'month', facts, status, onApply: apply, note: Text('Pets considered') })
SaleActionCard(home.price, { mortgageEstimate, onPressMortgage, onMakeOffer }).loading(sending)
MortgageCalculator({ defaultPrice: home.price, formatCurrency, disclaimer: Text(terms) }).style('split')
```

Bloom's `listing-actions`. Bloom's listing `ActionBar` is `ListingActionBar(price,
primaryLabel, options?)` (the language's `ActionBar` is the engagement bar):
`.loading()` spins its primary button and `.disabled()` disables it; its
`bottomInset` is a space token. `RentalActionCard(price, options?)` and
`SaleActionCard(price, options?)` take a listing's words, facts, status and
handlers as Bloom does, `.loading()`, their `note` and `footer` language
children. `MortgageCalculator(options?)` takes its values controlled or from
their defaults, as Bloom's does; `.style('auto' | 'stacked' | 'split')` is its
layout, its `disclaimer` a child, its `maxWidth` a space token. Bloom's
`computeMortgage` stays Bloom's.

### Listing cards

```ts
ListingCardGrid(homes.map((home) =>
  ListingCard(home.title, home.photos, { subtitle: home.area, price: home.price, priceUnit: 'month', facts: home.facts })
    .style('vertical').onPress(() => open(home.id)).id(home.id),
)).density('compact')
WishlistCard(list.name, list.covers, { icon: HeartIcon, empty: Text('Nothing saved yet') }).tint('primary').onPress(openList)
```

Bloom's `listing-card`: `ListingCard(title, photos, options?)` takes a
listing's words, price, facts, offerings and favourite as Bloom does, its
`badge` a language child; `.style('vertical' | 'horizontal')` is its layout,
`.density('comfortable' | 'compact')` its density, `.loading()` its
skeleton; `.onPress()`, `.onLongPress()` and
`.label()` are its own. `ListingCardGrid(cards)` lays the cards out (a list
keyed by `.id()`), `.density('results' | 'compact')` its columns.
`WishlistCard(name, photos, options?)`: `.tint(role)` its colour, its `empty`
a child, `.onPress()` its own.

### Listing editor and offering badge

```ts
ListingPreviewPane(draft, { renderPage: (listing) => ListingPage({ listing }) })
ListingQualityMeter(checklist, { title: 'Listing quality' })
PropertyTypeSelector([type, setType], { error }).columns(3)
OfferingBadge('sale').size('sm').style('onMedia')
```

Bloom's `listing-editor`: `ListingPreviewPane(listing, options?)` shows the
draft as a card or a page (its `mode` and handler Bloom's), its `renderPage`
a function of the listing returning a view; `ListingQualityMeter(items,
options?)` takes Bloom's items; `PropertyTypeSelector(type, options?)` writes
the type its binding holds, `.columns(n)` and `.disabled()`. Bloom's
`listingQualityScore` and `DEFAULT_PROPERTY_TYPES` stay Bloom's.
`OfferingBadge(offering, options?)` is Bloom's `offering-badge`: `.size('sm' |
'md')` (by default the nearest appearance scope's), `.style('tinted' |
'onMedia')`.

### Price insights

```ts
PriceEstimate(estimate.low, estimate.high, estimate.confidence, { asking: home.price, format: euro, reasons })
PricePerAreaComparison(rows).label('Price per m², by area')
```

Bloom's `property-insights` price pieces: `PriceEstimate(low, high,
confidence, options?)` takes the asking price, words, reasons and handlers as
Bloom does; `PricePerAreaComparison(rows)` takes Bloom's rows, `.label()` its
name.
### Agent editor, identity and profile

```ts
AgentCreator([agent, setAgent], { voices, onClose: close, sections: AgentCreatorSection('Memory', rows) })
AgentCreatorField('Description', TextEditor([text, setText])).multiline().divider(false)
AgentReplyIdentity(agent.avatar, agent.name, { thinking, reasoning: Text(thought) }).onPress(open).label('Open Ana')
AgentProfile(agents, { expanded }).onPress(openMenu)
AiProfileCard('Maya Collins', { handle: '@maya', stats, cells, actions: Button(Text('Follow')) }).tint('primary')
```

Bloom's `agent-creator`, `agent-reply` and `ai-profile-card`, as Gemies draws
them. `AgentCreator(agent, options?)` edits the agent its binding holds (its
`children` rows and its `sections` are language children);
`AgentCreatorField(label, content)` is one of its rows, `.multiline()` the
taller row with its label at the top, `.divider(false)` a card's first row
with no hairline above it; `AgentCreatorSection(title, content)` a titled
card of rows (a list keyed by `.id()`). `AgentProfile(agents, options?)` and
`AgentReplyIdentity(character, name, options?)` take `.onPress()`
themselves, `.label()` naming the press; an identity's `reasoning` is a
language child. `AiProfileCard(name, options?)` takes Bloom's data and
handlers, `actions` a language child, `.tint(role)` its activity grid.

- The editor's emotion ring turns on every frame: its cases are compared by
  Jest as they mount, not in Chrome.

### Order status

```ts
OrderStatusBar('Out for delivery', { eta: 'Arrives 14:35', progress, icon: BikeIcon, action: Button(Text('Call')) }).tint('success')
OrderStatusTimeline(order.steps).axis('horizontal').density('compact').label('Status')
```

Bloom's `order-status`: `OrderStatusBar(status, options?)` is the live strip
(its ETA, detail and progress as Bloom takes them; `leading` and `action`
views), `.style('surface' | 'plain')` its look and `.tint()` its tone, one of
Bloom's accent tones. `OrderStatusTimeline(steps, options?)` takes Bloom's
`OrderStatusStep`s (a step's own tone is its data); `.axis()` lays them out
(`vertical` by default) and `.density('comfortable' | 'compact')` its
rhythm.

### Lockup

```ts
Lockup(app.name, { subtitle: app.tagline, icon: app.icon, caption: Rating(app.rating), action: Button(Text('Get')).style('subtle').size('sm') })
  .size('md').shape('squircle').divider().onPress(() => open(app))
LockupSkeleton({ caption: true }).size('md')
```

Bloom's `lockup`: `Lockup(title, options?)` (its subtitle, icon, rank and
`href` as Bloom takes them; `caption` a string, drawn in the caption style,
or a view; `action` a view), `.size('sm' | 'md' | 'lg')`, `.shape()` its
icon's outline, `.divider()` a hairline under its text, `.lines(n)` where the
title truncates; `.onPress()` and `.label()` are its link's own.
`LockupSkeleton(options?)` takes `.size()` and `.divider()`.

### Ambient photo

```ts
AmbientPhoto(photo.url, { fadeMs: '2500', drift: settings.drift, onShown }).frame({ width: 'full', height: 'full' })
```

Bloom's `ambient-photo`: `AmbientPhoto(source, options?)` cross-fades to each
new photo and drifts while it rests; its timing is Bloom's (`drift`,
`driftScale`), its `fadeMs` and `driftMs` duration tokens. It moves on a timer, so
its cases are compared by Jest as they mount, not in Chrome.

### Month calendar

```ts
CalendarView(events, { title: t('calendar'), actions: Legend(), onNewEvent }).month([month, setMonth]).density('compact')
CalendarViewHeader(month, { onPreviousMonth, onNextMonth, onSelectDate, headingLevel: 2 })
CalendarViewMonthGrid(month, events, { showEventDetails: false, onSelectEvent })
```

Bloom's `calendar`: `CalendarView(events, options?)`, its header
`CalendarViewHeader(month, options)` and its grid `CalendarViewMonthGrid(month,
events, options?)` take Bloom's `CalendarViewEvent`s (an event's colour is
Bloom's named tint, its data) and handlers; `breadcrumb` and `actions` are
views, the month switcher's width a space token, and `.density('compact')`
the short day rows of an embedded preview. A highlighted day pulses on a
timer: compared by Jest, not in Chrome.
### Agent chats

```ts
AgentChat(messages, { title, status, onSubmit: send, threads, showHistory: true, headerLeading: Back() }).titleAlign('center')
AgentChatHistory(threads, { after: Help() }).selection([openId, open]).surface(false)
SelectedAgentPreview(chosen, { flights, anchor: 'picker', caption: true })
MultiAgentChat({ onRespond: reply, storageKey: 'workspace' })
```

Bloom's `agent-chat` (Astro's) and `multi-agent-chat` (Gemies' preview, the
site's workspace). `AgentChat(messages, options?)` takes its state, draft,
handlers, history and words as Bloom does; `emptyState`, `headerLeading` and
`headerIdentity` are language children; `.titleAlign('start' | 'center')` its
header's title, `.selection([id, setId])` the open thread of its history (a
binding: Bloom's `activeThreadId` and its `onSelectThread`).
`AgentChatHistory(threads, options?)`: `after` a child, `.selection([id,
setId])` the open thread (Bloom's `activeId` and its `onSelect`),
`.surface(false)` on a surface of the host's (Bloom's `framed={false}`).
`SelectedAgentPreview(agents, options)` takes the app's avatar flights
(`useAvatarFlights()`) and the name its avatars fly by;
`MultiAgentChat(options?)` the whole workspace's data, storage and handlers.

### Addresses and payment methods

```ts
AddressRow(place.title, { subtitle: place.line, kind: 'saved', action: Button(Text('Edit')) }).density('compact').onPress(choose)
AddressList(sections, { selectedId, onSelect }).style('picker')
PaymentMethodList(cards, { selectedId, onSelect, onAdd }).style('picker').loading(fetching)
```

Bloom's `address` and `payment-method`: `AddressRow(title, options?)` (its
`leading`, `badge` and `action` language children; `leading: null` draws no
media, as Bloom's does) and `AddressList(sections, options?)` (its `empty` a
child); `PaymentMethodMark(options?)` (its `image` a child),
`PaymentMethodRow(options?)` (its `image`, `leading` and `action` children)
and `PaymentMethodList(methods, options?)` (its `empty` a child). The options
are Bloom's data as it is: the selection (`selected`, `selectedId`,
`onSelect`), a row's `role`, the words. A list's `onSelect` stays its event,
not a binding: Bloom's one handler is also the row press of a read `list` (a
search result opened, nothing selected), and `selectedId` is read only by a
`picker`.

- `.density('comfortable' | 'compact')` is the family's density; a list's `.style('list' | 'picker')` is whether it is read
  or one entry chosen; an address's `.size()` a control size. `.disabled()`
  and a list's `.loading()` (its placeholder rows).
- The rows take `.onPress()` themselves.

### Prices and payment status

```ts
PriceSummary(order.lines, { total: order.total, collapsible: true }).label('Price')
PriceSummaryLine('Promo', { amount: '−€3.00' }).tint('discount')
PaymentStatusBlock(payment.state, { amount, reference, actions: Button(Text('Try again')) }).style('plain')
```

Bloom's `price-breakdown`: `PriceSummary(lines, options?)` (Bloom's own lines,
a line's `info` a React element as it is data; its total, disclosure and
words) and `PriceSummaryLine(label, options?)` (its amounts and state; its
`info` a language child), `.tint('default' | 'discount' | 'muted')` the
line's tone. Bloom's `payment-status`: `PaymentStatusBar(state, options?)`
(its `action` a child) and `PaymentStatusBlock(state, options?)` (its
`actions` and `illustration` children; `illustration: null` draws no glyph
tile), each `.style('surface' | 'plain')`.

### Cart and menu items

```ts
CartPanel(vendor.name, basket.lines, { summary, onCheckout }).tip([tip, setTip], TIPS, { label: 'Tip' }).promo([code, setCode], { onApply: apply }).density('compact')
CartPromoField([code, setCode], { onApply: apply, applied: basket.promo })
CartTipPicker([tip, setTip], TIPS, { label: 'Tip' })
MenuItemRow(dish.name, { price: dish.price, diets: dish.diets, onAdd: add }).onPress(open)
```

Bloom's `cart-panel`: `CartLine(name, options)` (its price, quantity and
handlers), `CartPanel(vendorName, lines, options?)` (the vendor, the line
handlers, the minimum order and summary as Bloom's data; its `empty` a child;
`.tip(tip, choices, options?)` its tip row and `.promo(code, options)` its
promo row, each a binding as `CartTipPicker` and `CartPromoField` take it,
drawn into Bloom's `tip` and `promo`; without one, no row),
`CartPromoField(code, options)` (its binding the code typed) and
`CartTipPicker(tip, choices, options?)` (its binding the chosen tip, the
choices Bloom's `options`). A line's quantity in the panel stays Bloom's data
(`onLineQuantityChange(id, quantity)`): it belongs to an entry of `lines`, a
list Bloom owns. Bloom's `menu-item`: `MenuItemRow(name,
options)` (its price, photo, diets, heat, quantity and handlers) and
`MenuItemOptions(groups, options)` (the choices made are `.value([chosen,
setChosen])`, every group's in one record, which Bloom requires: a group's new
choice writes the record with it; its `header` a child).

- `.density('comfortable' | 'compact')` on a cart line, the cart and a menu
  row; `.disabled()` on the promo field, the tip picker and the options.
- A menu row takes `.onPress()` itself.

### Dashboard cards

```ts
ImportantAlertsCard(alerts, alerts.length, { rangeLabel: 'This week' })
```

Bloom's fixed-height dashboard cards: `ImportantAlertsCard(alerts, count,
options?)` and `PatientInfoCard(name, details, options?)`. Their data is
Bloom's (an alert's tone and its `iconBackground` stay in the alert); a
`height` is a space token.



### Route stops

```ts
RouteStops(trip.stops, { onPressStop: edit, onAddStop: add, onRemoveStop: remove }).density('compact')
```

Bloom's `route-stops`: `RouteStops(stops,
options?)` takes Bloom's stops (a stop's `badge` and `action` are React
elements, as they are data) and its handlers; `.density('comfortable' |
'compact')`; `.label()` names the list.

`@oxy.so/bloom/mail-list` is live code with no component of its own: the
mail row's shared pieces. Its helpers, constants and types (`mailStrings`,
`groupMailByDay`, `resolveMailPaint`, `MAIL_ROW_GEOMETRY`, `MailSummary`…) are
what `List`'s mail recipe (`ListMail`), the mail thread, the composer and the
selection bar draw with, and its parts (`MailStar`, `MailGlyphButton`) are
inside those. The row is a node already, `MailRow(mail)` (Bloom's `ListItem`
mail recipe), and an app imports the helpers as plain functions (see
[Interop](#interop)).

## Generic components

Bloom's generic pieces no product family owns, each Bloom's own with parity on
native, web and Chrome, as the product components are: content first, data as
options, slots as language children, look as modifiers.

### Connection dots, outline nav, pagination and progressive blur

```ts
ConnectionDots(AppIcon(app), Logo(), { dotCount: 4 }).label('Linking')
OutlineNav(doc.headings, { activeId, onSelect: scrollTo, progress }).style('compact')
Pagination([page, setPage], pageCount, { siblingCount: 2 })
ProgressiveBlur({ direction: 'bottom' }).position({ bottom: 'none', leading: 'none', trailing: 'none' }).frame({ height: '64' })
```

`ConnectionDots(left, right, options?)` (`dotCount`, `reducedMotion`; its
`dotSize` a space token); `.label()` names the link. `OutlineNav(headings,
options?)` (`activeId`, `onSelect`, `progress`, `hideProgress`, `title`,
`compactMaxLevel`, `labels`): `.style('full' | 'compact')`.
`Pagination(page, totalPages, options?)` writes its binding, the page shown
(`siblingCount`, `previousLabel`, `nextLabel`, `getPageLabel`).
`ProgressiveBlur(options?)` (`direction` `top` or `bottom`, `intensity`) is
placed and sized by the view's own modifiers. Each is named with `.label()`
where Bloom takes a name.

### Row actions, swipe row and theme toggle

```ts
RowActions(file.name, { actions: [{ icon: DeleteIcon, label: 'Delete', onPress: remove }], menu }).style('outline')
RowActionButton(EditIcon, 'Edit').active(menuOpen).onPress(openMenu)
SwipeRow(MailRow(mail), { right: [{ key: 'delete', label: 'Delete', icon: DeleteIcon, tone: 'negative' }] }, { onAction }).tint('card')
ThemeToggle().style('segmented')
```

`RowActions(name, options?)` (`actions`, `menu`, `menuLabel`; its
`menuWidth` a space token): `.style('plain' | 'outline')` is its menu
button's look. `RowActionButton(icon, label, options?)` (`tooltip` and the
Button props it passes on): `.style()`, `.active()` while a menu it opened
shows, `.disabled()`; `.onPress()` is its own and `.label()` a fuller name
than its tooltip. `SwipeRow(content, actions, options?)` is Bloom's, its
drag on Gesture Handler as Bloom's is: the row is its content, `actions`
Bloom's panes per side (`onAction`, `onOpenChange`, `actionPresentation`,
`fullSwipeAction`, `closeLabel`; `height` and `actionWidth` space tokens,
`radius` a corner rung); `.tint()` is the fill the row travels on, a colour
role. Its `paint` stays Bloom's six colours (a set of roles has no prop to
paint). `ThemeToggle(options?)` (`transitionDuration`, a duration token): `.style('sidebar' |
'segmented' | 'sidebar-segmented' | 'bare-segmented')`, `.collapsed()` for
a collapsed rail.

## Interop

**JSX inside the language.** `Embed(element)` places any React element: a
third-party component, or a Bloom or app component the language has no node for
yet. `Embed((render) => <Link asChild>{render(Text('…'))}</Link>)` hands the
element a `render` function. That function draws language nodes in the current
environment as their host elements, so `asChild`, hover cards and slot props
receive a `Text` or `View` (its host, every prop, `ref` included, passed to it). An embed with box modifiers gets one `View`
around it.

**Bloom's helpers and constants.** What Bloom exports that draws nothing
(`computeMortgage`, `listingQualityScore`, `messageEntities`,
`DEFAULT_PROPERTY_TYPES`, `groupMailByDay`, a formatter or a table) is a plain
function or value, imported from its Bloom subpath and called in a view body
or a hook, as in JSX: `Text(money(computeMortgage(input).monthlyPayment))`. The
language makes nodes, so it adds no second name for them; their results are
what goes into a node's arguments and options.

**The language inside JSX.** `toElement(node)` returns a React element. A bare
`view()` node mounts its component directly. Anything else mounts under one
boundary component, which takes the environment provided where it is placed.
A root placed back in a language tree has no boundary: `Embed(toElement(chain))`,
an `Embed` render function or a `VirtualList` row that returns one, is drawn
as part of that tree, in the environment provided there.

**What a root costs.** Every node costs React what the JSX it stands for
costs: a press, a hit slop, a pinned edge, a fill at an opacity, an app colour,
a glyph (the per-node cost gate, `parity/cost.parity.test.tsx`, measures each
against its JSX). The boundary is the one thing JSX does not have: one fiber,
and a context read for each thing its tree takes from where it is placed. It
reads the environment (an `.environment()`, `.disabled()`, `.animation()`,
navigator or text trait set above reaches the root through it) when a node of
its tree takes from it: a press (a `.disabled()` above disables it), a text, a
glyph with no colour of its own, any modifier that sets a value for what is
inside it. It reads the theme when a node paints a theme colour; a fixed
colour (`white`, `black/60`, natively) and an app's own colour read none. A
tree of fixed geometry, a glyph in a fixed colour, an embedded element or a
`view()` (which reads what it takes itself) reads nothing, and a `view()`
inside it still reads what is set above. A RawText takes no trait set above it
(it writes only what its chain names), so it reads the theme only when a role
inks it; an `Avatar` or an `AvatarGroup` hands Bloom its props as they are (a
size, a shape, an avatar colour by name) and reads only what its box paints
(an AvatarGroup's ring in a role, its item action), so a `view()` that hands
its props to one costs what the component that renders Bloom's does (Mention's
`AccountAvatar`). What a tree reads is known from its nodes, once per node
(the modifiers that read nothing are the spec's `reads.nothing`), and read with
React's `use`, so a root whose tree comes to read more stays the same component
and keeps its state; a `view()` body reads the same way. A root whose tree is
host views alone (stacks and spacers, `reads.hostViews`) and reads nothing has
nothing for a boundary to provide: it is those views, with no fiber of its own
(Mention's splash marker, `VStack().frame({ width: 'none', height: 'none' })
.position({}).allowsHitTesting(false).onLayout(release)`, is the `View` it
replaced). Such a tree holds no component and no state, so one that comes to
read something is drawn again under a boundary, as JSX that comes to wrap a
view in a component is; a root that holds a `view()`, an embedded element, a
control or a list (whose items leave through a component of their own) keeps
its boundary whatever it reads. Mention's feed video preview (a tap area and a mute button
in one root) is its JSX's fibers, hooks and reads, and one read more, the
environment its presses take. A component whose JSX holds two roots pays them
twice; converting a region into one root, not each of its pieces into a root
of its own, pays them once, and a region already inside a language tree pays
nothing. Order still means nesting:
`.frame().background().border().corner()` is the one rounded, filled, outlined
view a JSX style writes; `.corner()` before `.background()` rounds the content
and fills a square behind it, a second view.

## ViewThatFits

`ViewThatFits(axis, ...candidates)` is SwiftUI's `ViewThatFits(in:)`: the first
candidate whose ideal size along `axis` fits the room it is given, else the
last. It is for content (a long translation, a count, a toolbar label); a
breakpoint stays `when()`.

```ts
ViewThatFits('horizontal',
  HStack(Text(pct), Progress(value).frame({ width: '96' })),
  Progress(value).frame({ width: '96' }),
  Text(pct),
)
```

The room is the box's own layout, and each candidate's ideal size its layout
in a hidden, unbounded, absolute row (or column) beside it, hidden from
assistive technology, so nothing wraps or shrinks it; until both are known the
first candidate is drawn. It is the same on every platform (on the web,
react-native-web's layout events). Each candidate is laid out once more, so
the compiler warns above three.

## Conditionals and lists

```ts
VStack(
  post.title ? Text(post.title).font('headline').weight('semibold') : null,
  post.media.length > 0 && Embed(<Media items={post.media} />),
  comments.map((comment) => CommentRow({ comment }).id(comment.id)),
)
```

## Animations

The animation layer is Bloom's motion engine (`@oxy.so/bloom/motion`, see
docs/motion.mdx) in the language's words: Bloom's own animations (the
engagement bar's pop and count roll, the toast's enter and exit, press
feedback) play on the same engine.

Motion is a token, like every other value: Bloom's motion table (`MOTION`) by
name, `press`, `present`, `dismiss`, `fade`, `slide`, `thumb`, `pop`, `roll`,
`enter`, `exit`, `restack`, `spin`, `pulse`, and the springs `select` and
`expand` (SwiftUI's `response` and `dampingRatio`); `spec.test.ts` holds the
spec's table equal to Bloom's. An app names its own with `defineTokens({
motion: { … } })`, which gives Bloom `MotionSpec`s. A reveal (an accordion's, a
collapsible's, a meter's `.revealMotion()`) takes a timed motion: every name but
the springs, or an app's own with a `duration`; Bloom eases a reveal and never
springs it. Curves are named too
(`ease`, `inOutQuad`, `outQuart`, `outExpo`, `backOut`, `linear`, and `pulse`,
Tailwind's `animate-pulse` curve `[0.4, 0, 0.6, 1]`).

| Modifier | Meaning (SwiftUI) |
| --- | --- |
| `.scale(s)`, `.offset({ x?, y? })`, `.rotation(r)` | Geometry that moves the view without moving its neighbours: `scale` tokens `dip` `pressed` `full` `swell`; offsets are space tokens (negative ones too); `rotation` `none` `quarter` `half`. Each takes a `MotionValue` in place of a token, and the view follows it. |
| `.animation(motion, value?)` | `.animation(_:value:)`: changes to this view's geometry and opacity, and what animates inside it (a matched geometry, a content transition, keyframes or a transition without their own motion), play with `motion`, the bodies of the `view()`s inside it included, as SwiftUI's transaction reaches its subviews. A variant's change plays too (`active:scale-95`). With a value, only on a render where the value changed. Colours change at once unless the view says `.animatesColors()`. |
| `.animatesColors()` | The view's colour changes (the fill, border, outline and text colour of its moving box) play with the `.animation()` in scope, its own or one above it: Tailwind's `transition-colors` (`hover:bg-muted transition-colors` is `.when('hovered', v => v.background('muted')).animation(m).animatesColors()`). The one way colours animate. A theme switch never plays: the switch's colours change at once (see How it runs). |
| `.transition(t)` | Insertion and removal: `opacity`, `scale('dip')`, `move(edge, distance)`, `t.combined(u)`, `asymmetric(insert, remove)`, `t.animation(motion)`. A half may be keyframes of the app's own. |
| `.keyframes(k, trigger)` | `keyframeAnimator`: plays `k` each time `trigger` changes to a truthy value (a boolean turning on, or a counter such as a press id). |
| `.repeatForever(loop)` | `keyframeAnimator(repeating: true)`: plays a loop again and again while the view is on screen: `'spin'` (a turn, 1 s linear: `animate-spin`), `'pulse'` (half opacity at the midpoint, 2 s: `animate-pulse`), frame for frame, or keyframes of the app's own. It plays when nothing else does, after an insertion ends; never under reduced motion. |
| `.delay(d)` | What the view plays waits `d` first (its `.animation()` changes, its insertion and removal, its keyframes, its loop): a duration token: Bloom's `instant` 100, `fast` 150, `normal` 200, `slow` 300 (ms), or an exact one the scale holds (`'0'`, `'800'`…). No app writes a Tailwind delay (the className scan has no `delay-*`). A delayed playing holds its first frame while it waits. |
| `.anchor(a)` | The point its scale and rotation (and its keyframes' and transition's) turn about; see [Modifiers](#modifiers). |
| `.phases(phases, trigger, build, motion?)` | `phaseAnimator`: through every phase and back, each time `trigger` changes to a truthy value (see below). |
| `.matchedGeometry(id, ns)` | `matchedGeometryEffect`: the view that takes `id` moves from where the last view with it was. `const ns = useNamespace()` within one tree; between screens, a namespace declared once at module scope, `const photos = transitionNamespace()` (SwiftUI's `matchedTransitionSource` beside a navigation transition): see How it runs. |
| `.contentTransition('roll', direction?)` | `Text` only: a change of the text inside an animation rolls the old text out and the new one in (Bloom's `rollKeyframes`, as `RollingText` rolls), up when `direction` rose and down when it fell. |
| `.contentTransition('numeric', direction?)` | `Text` only; `.contentTransition(.numericText(value:))`: a change inside an animation rolls each character that changed (the same `rollKeyframes`, each place its own pair on the engine) while the others stay put, matched from the end so a count gaining a digit keeps its units in place; a new character rolls in, a lost one rolls out beside the next. At rest it is the plain text; while it plays, a row with the text's box holds the pieces in its type, named by the whole text. |
| `.pressScale()` | Press feedback (`PressableScale`): eases to `pressed` while a finger holds the view. Off for a mouse on the web and under reduced motion. |
| `.drag(value, { axis?, min?, max?, onEnd? })` | `DragGesture`: the finger moves `value` along `axis` (default `y`) from where it was, within the bounds. Bind the value to see it; `onEnd` (once per touch: the value and the finger's velocity) decides where it goes, with `animate()`. |
| `.pinch(value, { min?, max?, onEnd? })` | `MagnifyGesture`: the fingers' spread scales `value` from where it was (a factor), within the bounds. Bind it to see it (`.scale(s)`); `onEnd` (the value and the pinch's velocity) decides where it goes. It holds the touch beside a `.drag()`. |
| `.onDoubleTap(action)` | `onTapGesture(count: 2)`: two taps in quick succession run `action`, on the gesture layer. A single tap is still the view's `.onPress()`. |
| `.swipe(direction, onSwipe)` | The view follows a swipe toward `direction` (`left` `right` `up` `down`) and, released past a quarter of the window across (or a tap's slop up or down), flies out and calls `onSwipe`; short of it, it comes back. |
| `Lottie(url)` | A node: `.loop()`, `.paused(bool)`, `.fallback(Image(still, label))`, `.frame({ width })` (its square edge: Sticker's `size`, no box around it), `.decorative()` (its container names it). Bloom's `Sticker` draws it (lottie-react-native on native, dotLottie on the web, both lazy), paused while its screen is not in front (`useScreenVisible`). |

Keyframes are frames, as CSS writes them:

```ts
const pop = defineKeyframes([
  { at: 0, scale: 1, easing: 'inOutQuad' },
  { at: 0.1, scale: 0.7 },
  { at: 0.4, scale: 1.2 },
  { at: 1, scale: 1 },
], 'pop');
Icon(liked ? LikeFillIcon : LikeIcon).size('md').keyframes(pop, press.on);
```

A frame's `easing` curves the stretch from it to the next; without one the
stretch takes the motion's curve, as a CSS animation applies its timing function
per stretch. Values are Bloom-internal numbers (a scale factor, an opacity, px or
a space token, degrees), as `defineTokens` allows.

**Phases.** `.phases(phases, trigger, build, motion?)` is SwiftUI's
`phaseAnimator`: the view rests in the first phase and, each time `trigger`
changes to a truthy value (a counter, a flag turning on), moves through every
next phase and back to the first. `build(view, phase)` sets a phase's geometry
and opacity with tokens, and `motion(phase)` the motion a step into that phase
plays with (Bloom's `select` spring by default):

```ts
Icon(liked ? LikeFillIcon : LikeIcon).phases(
  ['rest', 'lift', 'settle'] as const,
  likeCount,
  (v, phase) => v.offset({ y: phase === 'lift' ? '-sm' : 'none' }).scale(phase === 'lift' ? 'swell' : 'full'),
  (phase) => (phase === 'lift' ? 'pop' : 'select'),
)
```

**Keyframe tracks.** `defineKeyframes({ y: [...], scale: [...] })` is SwiftUI's
`KeyframeTrack`: each property (`x`, `y`, `scale`, `rotate`, `opacity`) its own
timeline of steps, each `{ to, motion?, ms? }` (a motion's curve and length, a
spring's physics included; `ms` alone is linear, with a motion it stretches it):

```ts
const bounce = defineKeyframes({
  y: [{ to: '-lg', motion: 'pop' }, { to: 'none', motion: 'select' }],
  scale: [{ to: 1.2, ms: 120 }, { to: 1, ms: 180 }],
});
Icon(StarIcon).keyframes(bounce, starred);
```

Both compile into Bloom's existing keyframes: phases are tracks (every
property steps through the phases together), and tracks are sampled at each
step's ends and eight points inside it, so each step keeps its own curve,
played linearly between the samples over the longest track. One keyframes
value per phases and motions, built once; the engine plays it as CSS
keyframes, with no JavaScript per frame, on every platform. SwiftUI's
per-frame `content` closure is not adopted. The compiler checks a phase's
build (geometry and opacity only, each token), and a tracks value is pure, so
a module constant holding one is static.

A `MotionValue` is the one way to tie a gesture to geometry, and apps never
write a worklet:

```ts
const y = useMotionValue(screenHeight);
VStack(Grabber(), content)
  .offset({ y })
  .drag(y, { axis: 'y', min: 0, onEnd: ({ value }) => animate(y, value > 120 ? screenHeight : 0, 'expand') });
```

`animate(value, target, motion)` eases it from where it is, with a token or a
spec (a spring keeps its physics). `useMappedValue(value, input, output)` is a
value that follows another through a range, piece by piece and held at the
ends, as data rather than a function, so an app still writes no worklet:

```ts
const y = useMotionValue(0);
const s = useMappedValue(y, [0, 300], [1, 0.9]);
VStack(Grabber(), content).offset({ y }).scale(s).drag(y, { min: 0 });
```

Native: Bloom's derived value (`useDerivedMotionValue`), worked out on the UI
thread in the frame its input moves. Web: written with its input, and eased
with the input's own animation (an `animate()` of the input transitions the
mapped view's style to where it lands). Nothing writes a mapped value, so it is
never a `.drag()`'s or a `.pinch()`'s. A bound view's geometry follows its values:
`.animation()` does not ease it, `animate()` does.

### How it runs

Gestures and bound geometry are Bloom's gesture layer (docs/motion.mdx, Motion
values and gestures): Gesture Handler 3's hooks writing the values from their
own callbacks (worklets on the UI thread on native) and the one mapper Bloom
writes; the web restyles the bound views directly and eases with CSS
transitions. The engine plays everything else (docs/motion.mdx, The motion engine): native on
Reanimated 4's CSS engine (CSS animations and transitions on an `Animated`
host, no worklet, no JS per frame), the web with CSS transitions and the Web
Animations API (no Reanimated). Press feedback is the one shared value on
native, read by the one mapper Bloom writes; a class on the web. Reduced motion
(`usePrefersReducedMotion()`) plays nothing, and a removal leaves at once.

A moving node is drawn by one component (`MotionNode`), so nodes without motion
stay plain elements with no hooks. Its outermost host becomes its animated twin
(`Animated.View`, `Animated.Text`, an animated `Pressable`) when it rests moved or
animates its resting state (geometry, opacity, a transition, a matched geometry,
press feedback, `.animatesColors()`, or, under its own `.animation()`, an
opacity or geometry it paints or varies), from the first render, because a CSS transition
needs its host before the change. A box whose variants answer its state is that
host itself (its `StateHost` draws the twin), so a pressed or hovered variant's
change plays where it is drawn. A node with only keyframes or a loop is animated
only while they play, as `EngagementPop` mounts its layer. A Bloom component (a
Surface, an Avatar, a bare glyph) cannot take motion itself, so it moves in one
box, which takes the view's place in its parent.

**Colours, loops and delays.** The moving box's transition covers its
`transform` and `opacity`, and with `.animatesColors()` its colours too
(`backgroundColor`, each border side's colour, `outlineColor`, `color`; on the
web `color`, `background-color`, `border-color` and `outline-color`, and a
glyph paints `currentColor`), the one transition Reanimated's CSS engine and
the browser play. A theme switch never plays a colour (docs/motion.mdx, A theme
switch never plays a colour transition). On native a view that animates its
colours reads the palette (`ThemedMotion`; no other moving view does): the
render a switch makes leaves the colours out of its transition, so
Reanimated's CSS engine sets them at once, and the render after puts them back.
On the web the provider applies the switch under a document mark that holds
such a host (`data-bloom-color-transition`) to its transform and opacity while
the browser computes the new style. A loop is the
engine's keyframes with an infinite iteration count (a CSS animation on native,
`element.animate()` with `iterations: Infinity` on the web), the lowest of the
one playing at a time, so it starts once an insertion ends and gives way to
keyframes; reduced motion stops it. A delay is the timing's own
(`transitionDelay`/`animationDelay`, the Web Animations delay), and a delayed
playing fills backwards.

**Matched geometry between screens.** A view matched in a
`transitionNamespace()` that arrives on a screen while another view with its
id is on the screen under it (a pushed screen: the list stays mounted beneath
the detail), or that replaces a screen the other just left (the web's routes
in place), flies from that view's frame to its own: Bloom's own flight, as the
avatar and media flights fly, on the engine. Its copy (the same node, drawn
again) is drawn in Bloom's `Portal` under an `OverlayRoot`, the layer over every
screen, at the view's frame, and plays the matched move there (the `moveFrom`
keyframes the in-tree move plays: Reanimated's CSS animation on native,
`element.animate()` on the web); the view itself waits, unseen, until the copy
lands. When the arriving view leaves (back) and the other is still on screen,
that one flies home from the leaving one's frame; if none is on screen, the
frame it left is kept for the next view with the id, once. The source is
measured when the flight starts (it may have scrolled since it laid out), and
the leaving view as it leaves, before its host is removed. It plays with the
arriving view's `.animation()` (none: no flight), never under reduced motion.
Reanimated's shared-element transitions (`sharedTransitionTag`) were not used:
in Reanimated 4 they sit behind a static feature flag the app must set, work
only between react-native-screens' native-stack screens (not Bloom's Android
scenes, not the web's routes in place), and the web cannot run Reanimated's
layout animations at all, so they would be a second mechanism for one
platform. The flight is measured, not run: no device was used here, and on a
pushed native screen the view's frame is taken where the stack lays it out,
which is where it lands.

**Scopes across `view()`s.** A `view()` placed inside an `.animation()` takes
the scope through a React context (`AnimationContext`), provided only where the
scope differs from the one already provided, as the environment is. A scope is
one object while its motion, value and gating are the same, so a view under it
re-runs its body when the scope changes (a gated value moved), not on every
render of the view that declares it; its nodes compare the scope's value with
the one they last committed, as the nodes of the declaring body do.

**Removal.** A list slot (`items.map(…)`) is Bloom's `Presence`, on every
platform: it keeps a child that left until its removal ends, holding its last
frame. Only children keyed by `.id()`
can leave: by index, a removal and a reorder look the same. A conditional child
(`show && view`) is a slot of its own, which keeps no record of what it held,
so it leaves at once: keeping one would put a `Presence` around every child of
every stack. A child whose removal should play is a list of one, keyed
(`[show && view.transition(t).id('x')]`), which is that list slot's `Presence`.

**What it does not play, and why.** There is no `withAnimation`: the language
animates by declaration, `.animation(motion, value)` on the view whose change
plays (its scope reaching the `view()` bodies inside it), not by a transaction
around a state write, since React batches and schedules renders apart from the
write, so a global transaction would have to guess which render it belongs to.
Tailwind's `animate-bounce` and `animate-ping` are not loop tokens: no app
writes them (the className scan has neither), and an app that wants one loops
keyframes of its own (`.repeatForever(defineKeyframes(…))`). `transition-all`
is `.animation(motion).animatesColors()`: geometry, opacity and colours; a
frame's size and a shadow change at once, as the engine moves and paints boxes
and does not lay them out again or redraw their shadows per frame. A colour
plays on the view's moving box (its outermost): a fill on an inner layer of the
same chain (`.background()` before a `.padding()`) and a text's ink inside it
change at once, and so does a native `Text`'s own ink, because each would need
an animated host of its own (Bloom's `Text` has no animated twin on native, and
a twin per layer would put a Reanimated host on every layered view); chain the
fill outermost (`.padding().background()`) for it to play.

**Glass.** On the web, an opacity below 1 on a glass host or any ancestor of one
flattens the material (AGENTS.rules.md, Material). The engine routes the fade of
a box that holds a material host: the box only moves, and the fade plays, with
the same frames, on the material's own layers and on each piece of content on
it. Native glass fades its own host. The compiler rejects a fade above glass
(`.transition(opacity)`, fading keyframes, a pulse loop or `.opacity()` on a
view whose children hold `.glass()`), since the renderer cannot route a fade it
does not own.

**Compiler.** `motion`, `scale`, `rotation`, `contentTransition`, `loop`
(`.repeatForever()`), `duration` (`.delay()`) and `anchor` values are token sets
it checks; `.offset()` keys and values are checked like `.frame()`'s; a string
where a transition or keyframes value belongs is an error. A `const` from
`useMotionValue` is a `MotionValue` in any scope: it may go in `.offset()`,
`.scale()`, `.rotation()` and as `.drag()`'s or `.pinch()`'s value; anywhere a token goes it is
an error, and so is a literal as their value, or one inside `when()`
(a variant varies geometry by token). A `const` from `useMappedValue` is a
`MotionValue` that only follows, so it is an error as `.drag()`'s or
`.pinch()`'s value. `.swipe()`'s direction is a checked token.
`opacity`, `move`, `scale`, `asymmetric` and `defineKeyframes` are pure, so a
chain that names them (`.transition(asymmetric(move('top', 'xl'), opacity))`)
hoists like any other static chain, and a module constant holding one is static.

### Bloom's animations on the engine

The engagement bar's pop (`EngagementPop`: the glyph's keyframes and the ring's
burst) and count roll (`RollingText`), the toast's enter and exit (`ToastRow`,
`animations.ts`; a row is kept by a `Presence` while it leaves) and press
feedback (`PressableScale`) play on the engine, at their old geometry, curves
and timings. The tab indicator follows the pager's drag, so it stays on shared
values, timed by the engine's table. Mention's language row draws Bloom's
`EngagementAction`, so its like gets the engine through Bloom.

Measured on a Pixel 8a, release builds of Mention (`feat/bloom-language-row`,
the JSX row) with the published Bloom 22.1.7 (old) and this line (new, at the
kept-mounted exit), interleaved, each run a cold start into a local fixture
screen outside the app's chrome, then the triggers; medians of 3 runs. Ticks
are 10 ms of CPU over exactly the triggers.

| | JS ticks old → new | UI thread | Janky frames old → new |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Like pop and count roll (30 likes) | 190 → 194 | 757 → 703 | 8 → 7 of ~1,480 |
| Toast enter and exit (8 toasts) | 94 → 107 | 477 → 481 | 5 → 4 of ~500 |
| Toast swiped away (8 toasts) | 88 → 113 | 553 → 555 | 0 → 0 of ~950 |
| Sheet dragged and let go (20 drags) | 44 → 114 | 1,074 → 1,103 | 24 → 2 of ~2,600 |

- **Toast exit.** A native layout `Keyframe` exit (gone at once, no JS) cost
  about one slow frame per exit (4 → 7 janky over the same 8 toasts, with or
  without a curve on its stretches), so the exit plays while the toaster's
  `Presence` keeps the row, as on the web: frames are level, at about 1.6 ms
  more JS per exit.
- **Swipe.** A swipe's gesture config is now one object for the row's life
  (it was rebuilt on every render of the row: +15 ticks over 8 swipes on the
  motion branch, +10 with the fix, at 240 ms a swipe). What is left follows the number of move
  events (+36 ticks when each swipe takes 900 ms): Gesture Handler 3's hook
  path hands every event to the JS thread as well (docs/motion.mdx, Motion
  values and gestures), while the same build with the old builder handler
  spends what the published Bloom does. The rest of the swipe's difference
  above is the kept-mounted exit of each swiped row.
- **Sheet.** Its drags on the gesture layer drop far fewer frames (the spring
  starts on the UI thread in the frame the finger lifts), at the same
  per-event JS cost of the hook path: about 3.5 ms of JS per 600 ms drag.
- **Tab drag.** Not compared: in the fixture (`RouterTabs` in a layout, the
  finger across the strip) the published Bloom's strip took no drag (40
  frames over 20 drags) while this line's moved the underline and switched
  tabs; that difference is behaviour, not cost.

On the web (headless Chromium, the web export, a phone viewport), the pop dips
to 0.7, swells to 1.2 at 120 ms and settles by 300 ms in both; the toast rises
and leaves the same distances at the same times, with no ancestor of a material
ever below opacity 1; the underline slides in a CSS transition (it no longer
starts two frames late); a touch drag moves the toast with the finger and
past the threshold it goes. Script time over the triggers (medians): like
59 → 46 ms, tab switches 42 → 36 ms, toast 47 → 50 ms, a toast drag 9 → 11 ms. iOS cannot be built
here: the native path is type-checked, not run.

## Mention's feed row

`Mention/packages/frontend/components/Feed/LanguagePostItem.tsx` (branch
`feat/bloom-language-row`) is `PostItem` + `PostHeader` + `PostActions` in the
language, behind `EXPO_PUBLIC_BLOOM_LANGUAGE_ROW=1`. The JSX row is the default.
A parity suite (`__perf__/languageRowParity.perf.tsx`) mounts both rows under the
real Bloom provider and compares every committed host node on four things: its
type, its text, its accessibility and press props, and its resolved style. It
covers ten fixture kinds plus the thread, reply-branch, repost, pinned, detail
and marker states. It allows three named re-spellings that draw the same pixels.

`BLOOM_LANGUAGE_COMPILER=1` puts the compiler in front of Metro
(`withBloomLanguage` in `metro.config.js`) and of the perf harness's Babel. The
parity suite passes both ways. In the row, the compiler hoists three chains (the
context-row spacer, the ⋯ glyph, the repost indicator) and marks four view
bodies; `TimeLabel`, `PostMenuButton` and `PostActions` are then memoized by the
React Compiler, which before compiled only `PostRow`. Most of the row is built
from runtime values and embedded JSX, so it gives the hoisting little to fold.

Measured on a Pixel 8a, release builds, 6 interleaved rounds (order rotated
ABC, BCA, CAB), each a cold start then 40 fixed drags; medians of n = 6:

| | JSX row | Language, interpreted | Language, compiled |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Janky frames | 1.10% | 1.94% | 1.56% |
| Frame time p50 / p90 / p99 | 8 / 11 / 18.5 ms | 8 / 11.5 / 20.5 ms | 8 / 12 / 19.5 ms |
| JS thread over the drags (ticks) | 5905 | 6026 | 5992 |
| Render thread over the drags | 2796 | 2921 | 2966 |
| JS thread idle, 30 s | 3710 | 3832 | 3689 |
| RSS | 958 MB | 930 MB | 894 MB |

The three are within run-to-run noise of one another (JS ticks span about ±3%
within each build). Two runs were disturbed and are in the medians: one compiled
run (the adb daemon failed mid-run; JS ticks +40%) and one JSX run (about 60%
more frames counted than any other). For this row, the compiler's gain is not
measurable on device: what it can fold is three small chains.

## className

27 of 29 apps style with NativeWind's `className`; the language replaces it.
[Bloom language and className](./bloom-language-classnames.mdx) is the
generated table of every utility the apps write, resolved to the modifier and
token it becomes or flagged, and `packages/bloom-language/codemod/classnames.generated.json`
is the same table for the app codemods. A value lands on a token only when a
language rung has exactly that value: a raw value with no rung is flagged with
the value, never carried as a number, and becomes a named token (a Bloom rung,
the owner's decision, or an app's `defineTokens`) before a codemod moves it.

- `node scripts/scan-classnames.mjs` (in the package) reads each app at its
  default branch, read-only, and writes `codemod/classname-usage.json`.
- `node scripts/generate-classnames.mjs` writes the table, its JSON and the
  summary below; `spec.test.ts` fails when they are stale.
- [The codemod](./bloom-language-codemod.mdx) (`packages/bloom-language-codemod`)
  reads the table, converts what lands exactly and proves each region with the
  parity harness.
